# TASKS — the queue

Live state of the loop.
Priority rule: **process blockers (`P-*`) outrank science tasks, and cheap bounds outrank expensive calculations.**
This overrides the default pull toward whatever has the best ROI.

Status vocabulary: `TODO` · `IN PROGRESS` · `BLOCKED` · `DONE` · `KILLED` (branch abandoned, with reason).

IDs: `T*` are the eight tasks of the problem definition §6, `P*` are process tasks we raised ourselves.
The `Leaf` column is the NDI `simulation-task-map` ID the work traces to.

**Read these five first.**

1. **THE FLATNESS NEGATIVE IS OVERTURNED, AND THE TILE §3 SPECIFIES IS ONE SOMEBODY HAS ALREADY BUILT.**
   Every structural claim before iteration 23 modelled a **2 nm** tile; §3 says *"~10 nm"*. On the tile §3
   actually specifies — **four honeycomb layers**, which one circular M13 pays for exactly — the free tile
   dishes ~~**0.0577199433** of the stroke **with no coupling at all**, inside `T-5b`'s 0.10~~, against the single
   layer's **0.307902368** (`C-0109`). ~~The threshold that verdict turns on is at `f = 0.0788618807` and the
   measured interlayer coupling clears it **monotonically by 3.29690337×** (`C-0116`).~~ **And the tile is
   design (i) of the caDNAno paper** — folded, gel-analysed, one of three of seven cross-sections to give
   sharp monomer bands (`C-0119`).
   **REPRICED, iteration 34 ([`C-0141`](gpd/claims/C-0141-honeycomb-station-lattice-and-placement.md), [`CH-0174`](gpd/challenges/CH-0174-the-four-layer-cross-section-is-not-a-honeycomb.md), swept under [`T-234`](gpd/tasks/T-234-honeycomb-correction-supersession.md)):**
   **the cross-section every four-layer number above is solved on is NOT a honeycomb** — every `edgeY` is exactly
   1.5× too small — and re-solved, `15 × 4` dishes **0.0978155002** free and **0.101759944** at the measured
   band's low end, which is **OUTSIDE `T-5b`'s 0.10**. Its threshold moves to **0.276970522**, *inside* the
   measured 0.26–0.33 band. **`10 × 6` stays flat (0.0240648102, threshold 0.012737738, 20× below the band),
   so the flatness overturning survives on `10 × 6` and not on `15 × 4`.** And the tile is buildable at **two**
   row lengths, not one ([`C-0140`](gpd/claims/C-0140-honeycomb-raster-turn-sense.md)).
2. **ITS OWN SOURCE RECOMMENDS A DIFFERENT CROSS-SECTION, AND ON THIS PROGRAMME'S CRITERION IT IS BETTER.**
   `10 × 6` — the same 60 helices — is **6.6× flatter** and has **no coupling-fraction threshold at all**,
   flat even at `f = 0` where the layers do not couple, **so it removes the one unmeasured number the
   `15 × 4` verdict rests on**. ~~The cost is **two-thirds of the footprint** (38.08 × 25.36 nm against §3's
   ~40 × 40), over which §3's 100 pN is specified and on which `C-0022`'s collar was **not** solved — a
   specification trade for NDI, not a free improvement (`C-0120`).~~
   **SUPERSEDED, iteration 34 ([`C-0141`](gpd/claims/C-0141-honeycomb-station-lattice-and-placement.md), [`CH-0174`](gpd/challenges/CH-0174-the-four-layer-cross-section-is-not-a-honeycomb.md)): THERE IS NO FOOTPRINT TO PAY.**
   On the honeycomb's own two pitches `10 × 6` is **38.08 × 37.504 nm** — essentially §3's square — and
   `15 × 4` is **38.08 × 56.524 nm**, 1.40× §3's 40.35 across. **The ordering reverses**: the cross-section
   this note charges a footprint for *is* §3's footprint, and the one it calls essentially §3's is half again
   too wide. Both criteria and the footprint now point the same way, and **the trade decision 7 asks NDI to
   accept does not exist.**
3. **AND A COUPLED TILE IS NOW FLAT UNDER THE MEASURED FOLDING STATISTICS, FOR THE FIRST TIME.** Of 16 graded
   cells at `C-0017`'s mandated total under `C-0087`'s dropout, **9 are flat at the 90th percentile** — all
   eight on `10 × 6`, best **0.0278431488** at the *sparsest* coupling tested. **The cross-section is worth
   3.17109774× and the distribution has no consistent sign**, so the design order runs tile first, coupling
   second (`C-0118`). The stations exist: the honeycomb top face supplies **90** on `15 × 4` and **60** on
   `10 × 6`, and snapping to its real 21 bp ladder keeps every flat cell flat (`C-0122`).
4. **THE TALL LAYER IS DEAD AND IT TAKES NDI'S OWN OBJECTION WITH IT.** §3's 100 pN stops arriving across a
   gap of **13.6989179 nm** at 0.5 mM — *below the bottom* of the 17–26 nm band NDI named — so a tall layer
   is refused at **96 of 96** states on the acceptable clause and admitted at **1 of 96** on the desired one.
   **It does not trade device A for device B: it loses both** (`C-0110`). So the one reserve NDI can spend
   once has **one** claimant, the buffer, and the remaining question is a **price** (`C-0114`).
5. **The electrode's unresolved quantity was a SIGN.** `E_pzc(Au(111)) = 0.46–0.51 V vs SHE`, 90–576× the
   deciding scale: an electrode at zero volt on the *rational* scale is negatively charged and **lifts** the
   tile. What is open is the **cell's** definition of zero — a one-line ask, filed under decision 3 (`C-0111`).

**Suggested order.** `C-0022`'s collar **re-solved at the `10 × 6` aspect ratio** — both `C-0120` and `C-0122`
name it, it is the load every four-layer number is read under, and it was solved on a 40 nm square. Then the
~~**oblique versus perpendicular root** (`C-0122`): half the top-face helices carry only oblique free azimuths
and this corpus has never priced one~~ — **SUPERSEDED, iteration 34: there is no perpendicular root anywhere on
a honeycomb face, every face helix carries exactly one rooting azimuth at 30°, and the cost is
2.67233333× rather than 6.017×** ([`C-0141`](gpd/claims/C-0141-honeycomb-station-lattice-and-placement.md),
[`CH-0175`](gpd/challenges/CH-0175-the-face-azimuth-is-thirty-degrees-and-there-is-one-of-it.md)).
Then `T-195` (the scaffold remainder, ~~now **1 344 nt** on the p8064 this
cross-section is actually folded from, not 529~~ — **the scaffold identity is NOT ESTABLISHED**: the caDNAno
paper's Methods list says p8064 and its own main text's rule says **p7560**, and the list agrees with the rule
at 6 of its 7 designs and disagrees only at ours, [`CH-0180`](gpd/challenges/CH-0180-the-scaffold-pairing-contradicts-its-own-paper.md)). ~~`T-50` and `T-9` remain the two genuinely expensive items and
both are named and costed rather than dodged.~~ **`T-50` is DONE as of iteration 32** (`C-0137`) — bounded by
a level/gradient decomposition rather than by the Monte Carlo, which was not run. ~~**`T-9` is now the only
genuinely expensive item left.**~~ **`T-9`'s FIRST deliverable is DONE as of iteration 35**
(`C-0157`, from the oxDNA run in `tools/oxdna/`): `k_θ` is bracketed at **5.62052112 – 25.9227606 pN·nm/rad**
and the fitted **13.5294118** sits inside it. Its **vertical-compliance** and **in-plane shear `k_s`** halves
stay open, and they are what `C-0015` and `C-0028` turn on.

**Harness.** `tools/verify.sh` for anything authoritative — ~21 minutes, and its document gates are now the
larger half of it. **Do not carry a remembered count of them**: `C-0171` measured the last one wrong in its
predicate and `P-29` found the newest one inert, so derive it —
`grep -oE '^\s+tools/[a-z0-9-]+\.py' tools/verify.sh | sort -u` returns **11 distinct scripts** at the time
of writing — 10 checkers and the reader census's own test — over **20** invocation lines, 5 of them
self-tests.
**Their self-tests are NOT all in the suite either**, and that is derivable too: seven checkers' self-tests
hang off `./gradlew test` and **three run only inside `tools/verify.sh`** (`check-corpus-identifiers`,
`check-entry-points`, `result-reader-census`), because those read the tree rather than fixtures.
And `./gradlew test` runs **only** self-tests, never a corpus gate: **a suite count is not a gate reading**
(`CH-0206`, `CH-0231`), and two claims recording *3 229* and *3 254* passing tests stood beside a checker
printing a defect on every run. `tools/reemission-order.py` is not a gate but **ask it before any re-emission
sweep**: a sweep is a topological *order*, not a list. **Reserve claim, challenge and task numbers in this
file before starting parallel agents.** **Three concurrent agents, not four** — four each running a `verify`
and a `study` OOM the Kotlin daemon despite the documented `-Xmx3g`, because that cure is per **daemon** and
the constraint is the **product**. And if a subagent launch fails twice on a transient API error, **do the
work in the main context** rather than relaunching; iteration 24 lost eight launches and completed all three
of its tasks that way.

**Three** agents this iteration, not four: iteration 23 measured four concurrent `verify` + `study` runs at
load 11.3 and 3 GB available, and the documented `-Xmx3g` OOM'd anyway because that cure is per **daemon**
and the constraint is the **product**. Nothing broke, and every study was half an hour late.

|---|---|---|---|
| E | `T-201` the fifth `ANSWERS.md` synthesis | `C-0115` | `CH-0132`, `CH-0133` — **both RELEASED UNUSED**, recorded here in iteration 39 because `T-268` later cited both as though they were challenges. Neither was ever filed; the findings they were cited for belong to [`CH-0154`](gpd/challenges/CH-0154-the-rule-lives-once-was-true-of-one-package.md) and to [`C-0138`](gpd/claims/C-0138-departure-rule-scope.md). *A reservation is not a claim on a number, and an unused one has to be released in writing* — this block is where that rule was written, and these two are what it costs when it is not applied |
| F | `T-196` the composite-fraction threshold | `C-0116` | `CH-0134`, `CH-0135` |
| G | `T-200` amend `C-0092`'s margin-movement deliverable | `C-0117` | `CH-0136` |

**`CH-0134`, `CH-0135` and `CH-0136` were never filed and are RELEASED UNUSED** — recorded in iteration 39, fifteen iterations late, by [`tools/check-corpus-identifiers.py`](tools/check-corpus-identifiers.py). The rule this block itself states — *a reservation is not a claim on a number; an unused one has to be released in writing, or the register credits a challenge to the claim that did not raise it* — was written in iteration 29 and applied to that iteration's numbers and to no earlier block's.

An agent needing more takes the next free number **above every block above** and says where it moved from.

## Number reservations — iteration 25 (2026-08-18)

Two agents, plus the coordinator. Iteration 24's lesson stands: three concurrent heavy runs, not four —
and after two transient API failures, an agent's work moves into the main context rather than being relaunched.

| Agent | Task | Claims | Challenges |
|---|---|---|---|
| H | `T-197` is a COUPLED four-layer tile flat under the measured dropout? | `C-0118` | `CH-0137`, `CH-0138` |
| I | `T-198` can four honeycomb layers be rastered from one circular M13? | `C-0119` | `CH-0139`, `CH-0140` |
| coordinator | `T-199` is 10 × 6 a better tile than 15 × 4? | `C-0120` | `CH-0141`, `CH-0142` |

**`CH-0137`, `CH-0138`, `CH-0139`, `CH-0140`, `CH-0141` and `CH-0142` were never filed and are RELEASED UNUSED** (`CH-0141` and `CH-0142` were re-reserved by iteration 26's block below and not taken there either).

**`T-199` is taken before `T-197`, deliberately, and the reason is worth recording.** `T-198` raised it
mid-iteration and it changes what `T-197` should be run *on*: `C-0109` already reports every coupled cell as
worse than the uncoupled tile, so `T-197`'s remaining value is confirming that two unspent axes do not reverse
a verdict the data already suggests — whereas `T-199` can move the **tile**, and running a coupling study on a
cross-section its own source recommends against is the more expensive mistake. `T-197` follows it.
`C-0118` and its challenge block stay reserved for `T-197`.

An agent needing more takes the next free number **above every block above** and says where it moved from.

and the second-pass tooling survey in [`TOOLING-NOVELTY.md`](TOOLING-NOVELTY.md),
which together produced the target layering in [`ARCHITECTURE.md`](ARCHITECTURE.md).
Layers 1–3 are **built and committed** (`9124037`) and **nothing calls them yet**, by design.
Rows `T-265`–`T-269` are the rest of that plan, in the order that file sets out;
`P-27` and `P-28` are what has to be true before any of them starts.

Agents are unassigned: this queue was written for a **cold start** and the reservations exist so that
whoever picks the numbers up cannot collide (`CLAUDE.md`: *re-listing `gpd/claims/` immediately before
writing does NOT prevent a collision*).
The highest numbers in use at the time of writing are `T-264`, `P-26`, `C-0157`, `CH-0205`.
`T-269` (the tools' extraction) is deliberately **unreserved** — it is a decision about where a
repository boundary sits, and it should not consume a claim number until somebody has decided it is
this programme's work at all.

**What setting this queue up already found, and already did.**
Two gates were red before any restructure work started, and only one of them is wired.

- [`tools/check-result-file-hygiene.py --prose`](tools/check-result-file-hygiene.py) is **wired into
  `tools/verify.sh` and exits 1** — that is `P-27`, and it blocks.
- [`tools/T-234-census.py --check`](tools/T-234-census.py) is **run by hand** and had drifted to **5 defects**
  at `HEAD`, measured on an unmodified `git archive HEAD` tree. **Three of the five were stale line numbers**,
  cured by regenerating [`tools/T-234-classification.json`](tools/T-234-classification.json) from its own
  rules, which is what that emitter exists for. Done in this pass; the gate now reads **1**, and the one that
  remains is the pre-existing `T-9` row at `TASKS.md:336`, which is a **square-lattice** statement the `WIDTH`
  family reads as a honeycomb premise — `T-262`'s open false-positive class, now with a second instance.
- **A hand override in that classification does NOT survive regeneration**, although the emitter's own
  docstring says it does: `T-234-emit-classification.py` builds its table from scratch and never reads the
  file it overwrites. Verified here by diffing the regenerated table against the committed one — the only
  content difference in the whole corpus was the occurrences this pass itself added — so **no override was
  lost, because there were none**. That is luck, not design, and it is `T-262`'s to fix or to record.

**Do not run `T-268` in the same pass as `T-265`–`T-267`.**
It re-emits the corpus, and the three additive steps change the emitters it would be re-emitting from —
which is `C-0101`'s consumer-before-producer error at the schema layer.

**Queue ownership, refined mid-iteration.** The coordinator owns `TASKS.md`, `JOURNAL.md`,
`ARCHITECTURE.md` and `CLAUDE.md` — **with one exception**: an agent may append its own
`Entry points` row, because [`tools/check-entry-points.py`](tools/check-entry-points.py) is a gate
and goes red the moment a new study is committed without one. A one-line table append cannot
collide the way a narrative row can. Everything else is still reported, not edited.

**ALL NINE WERE TAKEN — nothing to release.** `C-0167`, `C-0168` and `C-0169` are filed, and every one of
`CH-0213`, `CH-0214`, `CH-0215`, `CH-0216`, `CH-0217` and `CH-0218` was raised. The reservation line is kept
rather than deleted, because that is the record: it read *"reserved and not yet taken"* while the iteration
ran, and it says so now. Anything still unused when the iteration closes must be **released by name here** —
[`C-0166`](gpd/claims/C-0166-dangling-identifiers.md) found thirteen reservations released by nobody at all,
and that a release note written as a **range** does not release the numbers inside it. This line exists so
that [`tools/check-corpus-identifiers.py`](tools/check-corpus-identifiers.py) can see the reservation, which
is the point: **a number reserved and never named again is exactly the defect that gate was built for.**

**`T-272` is deliberately NOT in this block.** Its cost is measured — **≥ 7 h** for one pass over 148 files and
**≥ 14 h** with the by-kind control — and its own row says to schedule it as its own pass. Running it beside
three studies would put four heavy Gradle runs on a box whose ceiling is three.

**Why `T-9`'s vertical half and not its `k_s` half.** `C-0157` measured what the remaining deliverables cost:
the three plate rigidities need 100 independent samples per mode and got **20.5–24.2**, at **12–55 h per
replica**. The vertical/axial compliance is the half the row's own title says comes *"at no extra cost"* — the
trajectory that measured the roll is on disk. **Measure that claim before spending anything**; if the existing
run cannot answer it, say so and price the run that can, rather than shrinking the question.

The highest numbers in use at the time of writing are `T-274`, `P-28`, `C-0166`, `CH-0212`.

Raised `CH-0001`. |
| P-4 | Volume-fraction bookkeeping: locate the crossover for *this* layer rather than quoting φ ≈ 0.2–0.3 | **DONE** (iteration 2) | Answered, and the answer inverted the question: the binding crossover is the **dilute→semidilute** one at φ# ≈ 0.026, approached from *below*, not the semidilute→concentrated one. The layer sits at φ/φ# = 1.08–1.23. `C-0002`. |
| P-5 | Decide and defend the brush-regime criterion (`Σ ≥ 5` vs `Σ > 1`) | **CLOSED** (iteration 3) | **`L₀/R₀ ≥ 1` is exactly vacuous, not merely weak**: it admits **all 183** points of the Gen-1 sweep, including a layer at `Σ = 0.063` with 22 nm grafting spacing — asserted as a test in `C-0016`. **`Σ = πR₀²σ ≥ 1` is the only criterion that bounds anything**, and it owns the lower edge of every surviving window. **Four brush criteria have now failed in this project**, each a convention asked to do a measurement's work: `Σ ≥ 5` failed thermodynamically (`CH-0001`) and geometrically (`CH-0003`), and `L₀/R₀ ≥ 1` — adopted by `T-1c` to replace it — failed twice over, first as unable to bound from below (`C-0011`) and then as vacuous (`C-0016`). |
| P-7 | Build isolation for concurrent agents: `-PbuildDirectory=<dir>` so parallel runs of one checkout stop racing on `build/test-results` | **DONE** (iteration 3) | Raised as a process blocker mid-iteration: four agents sharing one working tree could not get an authoritative `./gradlew test`, and the failure (`EOFException`, `NoSuchFileException` on the in-progress results binary) looks like a broken test rather than a broken harness. `build-*/` is git-ignored. |
| P-10 | Verification isolation at high agent concurrency: `-PbuildDirectory` alone is not sufficient | **DONE** (iteration 3) | Raised by `T-8`, which lost **fourteen** full-suite attempts to it. `P-7`'s per-agent build directory is necessary but not sufficient — the Gradle **project lock**, `~/.gradle` and the Kotlin daemon are still shared, and the incremental compiler's session state races, producing `NoClassDefFoundError` on classes nobody touched. Fixed by [`tools/verify.sh`](tools/verify.sh), which runs the suite on an isolated copy of the tree (`--committed` archives `HEAD` instead, which is what the coordinator needs before pushing). |
| P-12 | Fold the multi-agent harness workarounds into `tools/` | **DONE** (iteration 4) | Taken **first** in a parallel iteration, overriding its own low ROI ranking, because `SESSION-PROMPT.md` puts process blockers above cheap wins and three concurrent agents were about to be started. [`tools/study.sh`](tools/study.sh) runs one study on an **isolated copy** and copies back only the changed `gpd/results/` files — `-PbuildDirectory` isolates `build-*/test-results` but **not** `build-*/classes`, and `T-1d`'s sweep is 33 minutes of that exposure. [`tools/verify.sh`](tools/verify.sh) gains `--drop <pkg>`, removing a package left mid-TDD by another agent — the **third** cause of `NoClassDefFoundError`, raised by `C-0015`. |
| P-11 | Collapse the two `χ` transfer conventions into one derived map — The source's SCF free energy read, replacing the 0.089 gap between the ratio and offset transfers with a derived relation | TODO — **low, non-blocking**. | Raised by `C-0013`. The Supporting Information (SCF equations) is paywalled at ACS and absent from the free NIST copy of the body. |
| P-13 | The electrode material and its potential of zero charge — a **specification** gap, not a modelling one | **ANSWERED** (2026-08-18, NDI — decision 3) — **TEMPLATE-STRIPPED GOLD.** *"Defaulting to template stripped gold for initial experiments."* The 2.6× material bracket collapses onto its **adverse** end: `C-0021`'s gold row (10.4–17.2 pN at 5 nm, well 4.82 `k_BT`; 0.74–1.42 pN and 0.65 `k_BT` at 10 nm) stops being the top of a bracket and becomes the number, and the *"retardation is sourced for gold only"* caveat is discharged. **No verdict moves** — a `1/h³` force still integrates to a bounded potential and 0 of 54 states confine. **The PZC half is still open** and is re-queued as `T-193`. ~~TODO — **low cost, high leverage** | Raised by `C-0021`. §1 says "patterned electrode" and never says of what: metal against oxide is **2.6×** on the one hold-down that cannot be designed away (`C-0021`). And a contact potential of **0.9–5.1 mV** supplies the entire thermal-scale hold-down, which no §3 parameter fixes. **One question to Kazik, not a calculation** — carried to the open-questions section below.~~ |
| P-14 | The charge presented by the **cut rim** of a DNA-origami sheet | **DONE** (iteration 30) — claim [`C-0132`](gpd/claims/C-0132-cut-rim-charge.md), raises [`CH-0156`](gpd/challenges/CH-0156-the-rim-charge-bracket-is-a-bigger-tile.md) and [`CH-0157`](gpd/challenges/CH-0157-the-rim-sweep-varied-the-smaller-of-two-terms.md) | **The rim's areal charge density is `σ_face/2 = −0.199332619 e/nm²` — exactly one half, at every slab with `t ≤ 2a`, carrying no `ρ`, no `t`, no buffer and no Manning fraction — and knowing it does not close `C-0022`'s bracket, because the model has already spent that charge on its faces.** `σ_face = ρt/2` is Gauss's law on a slab, not a convention, so a smearing is a partition of ONE conserved charge onto ONE boundary and the rim's area is exactly half the faces'. **`C-0022`'s falsifier 5 is therefore not a reading of the rim: it applies `1.5000` of the tile's charge in 3-D and `1.2500` in 2-D — a bigger tile — and the `1.845×` bracket is WITHDRAWN rather than narrowed** (`CH-0156`, one division, no solver). The conserving family is one-parameter in the face taper `ℓ`, `σ_rim = ρℓ/2`, whose `ℓ = 0` member IS `C-0022`'s headline; nearest-surface picks `ℓ = t/2`. But a taper has no counterpart in the sheet — the column behind every face element is full thickness to the rim — and it moves charge across a 90° corner onto a wall that exerts no vertical force, so **`σ_rim = 0` in this model is FORCED by `σ_face = ρt/2`, and `C-0022`'s headline survives on a ground it was not published on**. What replaces the bracket is a model span read on the collar, because the fitted `(depth, width)` pair **degenerates** under a face taper (deficit sign changes 3 against 1, width `1.43` of the half-width and **−12.97 nm** at `ℓ = 3t/4`, and it does not converge while the collar does at second order, ratios 3.85/3.84 — `CH-0157`): **1.222623–1.77269012 nm, `1.44990738×`, STRADDLING the headline 1.65495953 nm**, against a published one-sided 1.65495953→2.91297923 nm, `1.76015133×`. The unswept term is **1.41×** the swept one and runs the other way. The saturation cheap bound ran first and **did not settle it** — halving the bare rim charge costs 6.8 % of its far-field effective charge, quartering it 18.7 % — because zero is the family's boundary, not a point inside it. The two rims are different objects and **one** density (0.1139 duplex ends per nm², 35.8 % coverage, against continuous sidewalls), separated only by a charge **depth** of **0.0913621415 nm** that exists solely because `T-71` MEASURED the phosphate radius; and **neither rim is cut**. The ratio transfers to `C-0109`/`C-0120`'s four-layer tile exactly, discharging its *"`C-0022`'s charge … is not re-derived here"* note for the RATIO and leaving it open for the SOLVE. No `C-0022` verdict moves; **fourteen** downstream claims carry an exposure that is too large and pointed the wrong way. Falsifier `F3` FIRED and is recorded. Result `gpd/results/P-14-cut-rim-charge.json`, re-run byte-for-byte identical |
| P-16 | `tools/verify.sh --drop` at FILE granularity | **DONE** (iteration 5) | Taken **first**, ahead of every science task, because three agents were about to be started against one checkout. `--drop-file <repo-relative path>` added to [`tools/snapshot.sh`](tools/snapshot.sh) and wired into both [`tools/verify.sh`](tools/verify.sh) and [`tools/study.sh`](tools/study.sh), with `--drop <pkg>` demoted to the fallback it should always have been. Both deleting helpers now carry executable tests — [`tools/test-snapshot.sh`](tools/test-snapshot.sh), 19 checks — which is the discipline the Kotlin side already runs under, applied to the harness that protects it; `drop_files` additionally refuses an absolute path, a `..` escape and a directory argument, none of which a *package* name could express. **A second harness fix travelled with it**: `kotlin.daemon.jvmargs=-Xmx3g` moved into the checkout's own `gradle.properties`, so every snapshot inherits the documented cure for the daemon OOM rather than each agent remembering to patch its copy. |
| P-15 | Repair `bracketedRoot`'s Illinois sign test | **DONE** (iteration 5) — claim `C-0031`, raises `CH-0043` | **There were two defects and the reported one is not the one that was costing anything.** The product sign test is real — and it also has a second half `C-0019` did not name, the *entry* test accepting a bracket with no root — but a product underflows only once **both** factors are below `1.5e-154`, and a direct probe of the unrepaired routine on residuals spanning 30 decades escapes **zero** times. What was active on **every** call is that **the Illinois halving was unconditional**: Dowell & Jarratt (read directly) halve only when the *older* endpoint is retained, and halving every step deflates both residuals so they interpolate to the midpoint — the method silently became **bisection while still paying for a secant**, 52 evaluations against bisection's ~52 on `x²−2` where correct Illinois takes **11**. The doc comment's "eightfold saving" was the whole justification for the method and it was delivering a *loss*. The two are coupled: the halving is the **amplifier** of the underflow. Diagnosis closes as a **theorem** — a secant through opposite-signed ordinates is a convex combination, so an escape *proves* a lost bracket. **Re-run and diff: no verdict, no window edge and no quoted figure moves anywhere.** Movement is one of exactly three things — one ulp (`T-1c`: 11 281 of 11 336 fields at a median `4.3e−16`), the SCF's own declared `HEIGHT_TOLERANCE = 1e-6` (`T-1f`: 589 fields, median `9.0e−7`), or a quantity that is **identically zero** (`T-1c`'s `equilibriumStiffness` at `L₀`, `\|value\| ≤ 2.0e−11 pN/nm`; `T-16`/`T-23`'s reproduction residuals at `~1e−9`). |

| P-8 | Mg²⁺/PEG coordination constant in water | TODO — medium | Raised by `C-0005`. PEG's ether oxygens coordinate cations — the mechanism behind PEO polymer electrolytes — and this is the **only** mechanism that could flip the sign of the §4(c) answer, since `T-6`'s partitioning bound counts exclusion only. **`P-6` searched independently and confirms the number does not exist in accessible literature**: the mechanism is stated for the right system in water, with MgCl₂ in the salt list, but no constant, and the quantitative multivalent-cation/PEO NMR work is in **methanol**. Needs a paywalled pull or an experiment. |
| P-9 | **The effective `χ` of a *grafted* PEG layer is not the bulk one** — bound it, or declare `C-0002`'s bulk equation of state inapplicable to a brush | **DONE** (iteration 3) — **(a) inapplicable**; `C-0002`'s bulk equation of state **stands** | Claim `C-0013`, challenge `CH-0012`. **`χ ≈ 0.60` is not in the source.** Its fits are 0.789/0.852 on a scale whose *own* theta is **0.696** — the 0.60 was `1.2 × ½`, the ratio transferred onto the wrong axis, and the paper's disclaimer forbidding exactly that step is on the same page as the number. The system is an **air/D₂O Langmuir monolayer under *lateral* compression**, not a solid-grafted layer under normal compression. Its grafting densities **do** overlap the Gen-1 window, so the task closed on the *parameter*, not on system mismatch. Bounded independently at **`\|Δχ\| ≤ 0.053`** from normal-compression fits on grafted PEG at 1.5–2.5× the Gen-1 density: −11.4 % to +4.3 % in stiffness, inside `C-0003`'s own ±22 % bracket. |
| P-6 | `χ(T, salt)` and the Mg²⁺ salting-out coefficient for PEG/water at 2–10 mM | **DONE** (iteration 3) | Claim `C-0007`, challenge `CH-0006`. **The buffer does not reach the layer's mechanics**: the mobile-ion channel is exactly zero, and the solvent-quality channel is ≤ 0.4 % of the modulus over 2–10 mM. The Mg²⁺ coefficient is **not determinable and probably not well posed** — `θ(c)` shows *minima* for Group II chlorides, and PEG forms no binodal with MgCl₂ at all, so no ATPS-derived coefficient can exist. Bounded by a threshold instead: MgCl₂ would need `k_s ≥ 92.8 K/M`, 1.35× above the ceiling any PEO salt reaches. |

The governing variable is the attachment's **distance to the nearest crossover**, and the duplex shear runs the *opposite* way along it. **Flatness needs 45 attachments as 3 × 15, not 64 as 8 × 8** — `C-0009` searched the square diagonal of a two-parameter space on a 25.6×-anisotropic sheet. **One attachment row per duplex zeroes the per-path force exactly.** |

Costed against `C-0022`'s **solved** edge profile the exact zero becomes **0.150 pN** (0.332 worst of 21 states, 0.105–0.166 over the `k_f` sweep) — **6.8 % of the 2.222 pN static share and 67× below unzip**. Attachment scatter is the non-uniformity a builder controls, **0.883 pN per unit relative amplitude**, overtaking the edge at ε = 17 %; and it is **exactly zero** if the scatter alternates along the helices instead of across them. **Even at ε = 0.99 (0.86 pN) or the worst grid in the sweep (2.42 pN) the crossover path is 4–12× clear** — it never becomes binding, and the binding constraint stays the static share `100/n`. **Thermal excitation is not a member of that family at all**: it is `√(k_BT k_v)`, present under a uniform load, scheme-independent, and it **diverges as `√k_link`** — `T-9`. One grid discharges all three duties (lateral 60×, yaw 19×, hold-down 72×); **rows set by the load path, columns by flatness**. The zero is retired as a headline and kept as a free **20.2×**. |
| T-45 | The assembly tolerance of a 45-path DNA coupling | The relative stiffness scatter a staple-designed attachment array can be guaranteed to, against `C-0026`'s 0.883 pN per unit amplitude and its 17 % break-even | A1.2 | **ANSWERED as far as published measurement allows** (iteration 14) — [`C-0072`](gpd/claims/C-0072-plan-tolerance-model.md), [`CH-0084`](gpd/challenges/CH-0084-the-measured-staple-incorporation-is-past-the-flatness-threshold.md). **The measurement existed the whole time, under a different name.** `T-134`'s survey (77 recorded queries) found **Strauss et al., *Nat. Commun.* 9:1600 (2018)** — a DNA-PAINT map of incorporation efficiency at **single-staple resolution across all 168 staples** of a Rothemund rectangle, corroborated by next-generation sequencing: *"absolute incorporation efficiencies of **48–95 % with an average of 84 %**"*, **strongly position-dependent, worst at the edges**. A missing staple does not perturb a load path, it **removes** it, so the population is Bernoulli and `σ_rel = √(f/(1−f))`: the mean gives **43.6 %** relative per-path stiffness scatter — **1.26×** `C-0060`'s 34.6 % flatness threshold and **2.57×** `C-0026`'s 17 % break-even — the edge sites give **104 %**, and the centre sites **22.9 %**, which is inside both. **The mean alone, with no pattern assumption, is a 16 % shortfall on `C-0017`'s mandate**, 2.9× the worst rounding placement error `C-0060` spends a trim to remove. **And the position dependence runs the wrong way for `C-0058`**, which puts 34 of its 45 stations on the rim and gives them the stiff level. What is **still missing** is the stiffness spread of nominally identical *hybridised staple extensions* as such — nine queries across three databases found nothing, and the nearest published work (Videbæk et al., *PNAS* 2025) measures an inter-subunit ssDNA handle by cryo-EM multi-body refinement, which is the method that would supply it one level down. **What would close it**: a per-site incorporation measurement on a coupling-bearing tile (routine, by Strauss's own method), and `C-0060`'s flatness sweep re-run under a **position-dependent Bernoulli dropout** rather than an alternating amplitude. ~~TODO — **medium, and cheap**.~~ **`C-0060` (`T-122`, iteration 10) supplies the THRESHOLD the measurement is graded against**: `C-0058`'s flat design loses `T-5b`'s verdict at **34.6 %** relative scatter (31.6 % with `C-0017`'s total held), **2.04×** `C-0026`'s break-even and **0.52×** the 66.7 % at which the two stiffness populations merely *overlap* — so flatness binds at half the amplitude the ordering does, and a small scatter *improves* the dishing. It also finds that `C-0026`'s **build rule reverses** on a non-uniform coupling ([`CH-0073`](gpd/challenges/CH-0073-the-along-helix-scatter-rule-reverses-on-a-non-uniform-coupling.md)). What is still missing is the measurement itself. `C-0026` reports the sensitivity per unit amplitude precisely so a measured or specified tolerance can be substituted without re-running, but nothing accessible gives the stiffness spread of nominally identical hybridised staple extensions. It is also the only non-uniformity a *builder* controls, and `C-0026`'s build rule — **let the scatter alternate along the helices, not across them, where it restores exactly zero** — is worth stating in a design document whatever the number is. |

The axial bracket is decided by **anisotropy, not softness**: every covalent motif is isotropic, so a **direct crossover** gives span 44.03 nm, tangent **79.18 pN/nm — 1.98× past the 40 pN/nm ceiling — at every one of `k_s`'s four decades** and 61.0 pN of beam tension at §3's desired stroke. **`C-0023`'s 2 nt ssDNA hinge is falsified** (`CH-0031`): a flexible link has no direction, so it buys 1.30 nm of draw-in for **1.30 nm of transverse dead band**, 43 % of the stroke. **A double nick IS a crossover**, to the last digit. **The draw-in factor is 2.4 only at the endpoints** — interior minimum exactly **9/4 at ρ = 8**. **The design: 45 flexures, span 31.64 nm = 93 bp, on 8 nm = 24 bp NORMAL standoffs** (anisotropy `Sℓ²/3EI` = 102×), tangent **37.39 pN/nm**, tension 0.37 / 3.83 pN at 3 / 10 nm; window ℓ = 7–10 nm. `CH-0029` inverted: the restrained reading asks 86.7 pN, **past the 68.1 pN saturation — no bonded length carries it**. **`T-13` still closes.** |

A softer base **relieves** the compliance ceiling (tangent 33.8–38.5 pN/nm over the whole 3–10 nm range) and **destroys** stability: a single-crossover base **buckles at all eight lengths**. A pinned base with a free head is a **MECHANISM**, `P_c = 0` exactly — a corner the "factor of exactly 4" bracket does not contain — and **the standoff's sway IS the flexure's draw-in**, so the held-head reading is unavailable. What closes it is **orientation**: two crossovers laid *across* the flexure give **261.2 pN·nm/rad** against **27.06** laid along, **9.65× free**. **Design: span 31.06 nm = 91 bp on 8 nm standoffs with a two-crossover favourable base**, tangent **36.51 pN/nm**, buckling **1.41×** free-head, window **`ℓ = 7–9 nm`**. **`CH-0037`**: `C-0025`'s duty is the *mandate* secant, 1.27–1.70× low. **LITERATURE: the motif is NOT established** — every published out-of-plane base is a **pin**, and rigidity comes from **triangulation**. Fields et al.'s measured duplex buckling implies `L_p = 41.7 nm`, **25 % below CanDo**, so every `P_c` here is optimistic. **`k_s` now moves a verdict** — the first time in this programme. |

So the beam is in axial **COMPRESSION** over `0 < s < 9.9 nm` (`C-0023`'s cable term changes sign), the duty at 10 nm falls **5.113 → 3.313 pN**, and the free-head margin rises **1.41 → 2.18×** (CanDo) and **1.06 → 1.64×** (measured). Against a *net* demand the joint is **2.06× STIFFER**, not softer. **`c₀ ≡ c(ρ)` exactly**; the *effective* `c` becomes a function of the **stroke** (124.4 at 3 nm, 92.2 at 10). **But `Φδ` is odd where `e(δ)` is even, so the law is no longer ODD and the sign is a MOUNTING CHOICE**: favourable gives window **`ℓ = 5–10 nm`**, adverse gives 42–61 pN/nm and **no window at any length**. New: the flexure has its own buckling condition (`P7`, 3.1–4.2×), and the favourable mounting costs a **clearance** — §3's desired 10 nm stroke unreachable at any `ℓ ≤ 10 nm`. Design: **span 31.82 nm = 94 bp on 8 nm standoffs, tangent 25.23 pN/nm, margin 2.18×**. Maxwell-Betti asserted between two independently integrated off-diagonals, departure `0.0`. |

The sign is a **PRODUCT of two binaries** — base body **and** standoff normal — of which `C-0030` named one: of the four mountings exactly two are favourable, **one with each body and one with each normal**, so "which body" predicts the sign no better than a coin. Equivalently: favourable ⟺ the tie **crosses the base plane** ⟺ the flexure is **outboard** ⟺ the standoff is in **COMPRESSION**. **The survivor is unique — `Su`: standoff bases on the OUTPUT SUPERSTRUCTURE, standoffs pointing AWAY from the tile, flexure outboard above it, each midspan tied back DOWN through it to the tile.** The other favourable mounting (`Td`) puts the flexure inside the actuation gap: **37–85 % of the layer's own volume**, and at or below the electrode at §3's 5 and 7 nm layers, 0 of 9 admissible. Both adverse mountings fail `C-0023`'s ceiling **and** cannot place §3's effort point — inboard it cannot come closer to the tile than `ℓ`, and §3 allows **5 nm at the 10 nm layer on both readings of its band**. **The pre-bow escape is real, priced and rejected 12 of 12**: 4.08–16.66 nm of rise (12–49 bp) for a **150–225 pN preload = 1.5–2.25× §3's whole target force**, and **CLOSED outright at the desired stroke**. **The tile now carries no out-of-plane element at all — only `C-0015`'s 45 tie attachments.** |

| T-119 | Can a flexure hinge be built on a junction site the single-layer sheet does not use? | A literature answer, or the statement that the motif does not exist — the one way `C-0054`'s exclusivity premise fails | A8.2 | **DONE** (iteration 9) — claim [`C-0055`](gpd/claims/C-0055-unused-junction-site.md), raises [`CH-0068`](gpd/challenges/CH-0068-the-hinge-inventory-is-not-the-sheets-own.md). **Answer (a): the site exists, it is published geometry, and `C-0054`'s pigeonhole falls — while `CH-0066`'s conclusion survives for a different reason.** Ke et al. (*JACS* **131**:15903, 2009, **read directly**): *"each double helix has up to four nearest neighbors … Every 8 bp, that staple strand is positioned to cross over to one of its four neighbors … adjacent helices share crossovers every 32 bp"*, and *"crossovers in i and iii … are parallel to the xz-plane, while … ii and iv … to the yz-plane"*. A single-layer sheet builds on **one of the two orthogonal plane families**; `8 bp × 33.75°/bp = 270.0°` **exactly**, so the empty family points **out of the sheet plane**. **The unused site is in BETTER register than the used one** — 4.286° against 8.571° at the preferred 10.5 bp/turn, exactly 2:1, because the departure is linear in the offset. **Census: 161–176 junction sites, of which the sheet builds 49–56 — it occupies 27.8–33.1 %, under a third at every phase**, and the in-plane half reproduces `C-0015`'s 56/49 as a **set equality** at all 32 phases from an independent construction. **So exclusivity AT A SITE is upheld and the INVENTORY premise is false**: the ceiling is not `56 − 14 = 42` with a severed tile but **52 upward hinges at `C-0054`'s own phase and 60 at the best, with all 56 interface crossovers retained and the sheet in ONE piece at every count**. **But the design count only moves `C-0053`'s 25 → 34**, because the escape and its price are the same fact — an upward line belongs to **one** duplex, so nothing empties *and* its roots sit at 10.88 nm against an arm demanding 11.82, three arms per row falling to two at `arm > 8.19 nm` (`n > 34`). **§3's 45 still does not place on a 40 nm tile — 30 do — for the root pitch, not for severance; at a 49.25 nm edge 45 places with the host intact.** New phase trade: the ten phases maximising the upward inventory (0–2, 14–18, 30, 31) are **disjoint** from `C-0015`'s ten that maximise the interface one. **The motif is half-published**: the site and a crossover on it are the elementary step of square-lattice multilayer origami, but a **free lever** held by one crossover there was **NOT FOUND** in 62 recorded EuropePMC queries across fifteen families, every published origami hinge is an **ssDNA** connection (2023 review, read directly), and Ke et al. name the **8 bp staple break** it forces as a *"significantly lower yield"* cost. Opens `T-121`. |
| T-120 | Is a sheet held together by ONE crossover per interface still a plate? | `C-0009`'s discreteness criteria re-derived at the connectivity ceiling | A8.2 | **DONE** (iteration 9) — claim [`C-0056`](gpd/claims/C-0056-connectivity-ceiling-plate.md), raises [`CH-0069`](gpd/challenges/CH-0069-the-plate-fails-before-the-connectivity-does.md). **NO, and it stops being one FIVE crossovers before it stops being connected.** The depleted lattice is parametrised by **one** number through `p_eff = p N/N_ret` and `D_⊥ = k_θ d/p_eff`, both exact at `N_ret = N`, so every criterion is `C-0009`'s published number times an exact power of `N_ret` and **each therefore INVERTS**. At the ceiling an anchor's influence patch holds **0.694** crossovers against 3.93 intact, and **inverted rather than evaluated the patch criterion crosses one at 37.3 SPENT — inside `C-0054`'s own `39 ≤ n ≤ 42` window.** The other two criteria are useless here: `ℓ_⊥/d` reads **1.061**, above one, and never fires; `ℓ_∥/p_eff` demands **67.2** crossovers, more than the sheet owns, so it is **degenerate** — it fails on the intact sheet at every foundation but the softest. **`ℓ_⊥/d` cannot tell depletion from a stiffer foundation at all** (both divide `D_⊥/k_f`; the ceiling's 1.061 IS `C-0009`'s `k_f×4` corner, exactly), where the other two separate the same pair by `2√2`. **A criterion is not a model**, so a third model — fifteen uncoupled beams on one foundation — was run beside the plate: **the four thresholds fall in a strict order, 28 (point compliance) < 37.3 (criterion) < 42 (connectivity) < 45 (smooth load), 1.61× apart.** `C-0009`'s smooth/point split **survives and becomes a change of model class**: the plate is out 14.9 % on `C-0022`'s solved load at the ceiling and **33–79 % under a concentrated lever**, and the point compliance varies **2.53×** over one cell where the plate says 1.14× — a quantity the plate does not have. `C-0006`'s load distribution stands (the hinges carry `9e−4` of the energy **intact**); **`C-0010`'s insensitivity survives and strengthens** (3.07 % → **0.99 %** at the ceiling) while the amplitude rises 1.71×, so it is insensitivity to the *rigidity* and not to the *connectivity*; `C-0047`'s 0.218/0.695 and `CH-0034`'s saturation are unchanged. **Found and bracketed: `C-0054`'s `SPREAD` tie-break clusters all 14 survivors into the two lowest columns** — a `staggeredRetention` is 1.68× better in the distance from an anchor to the nearest surviving crossover at identical count and density. |
| T-121 | What do 34 duplexes stacked ABOVE the tile do to it? | `C-0009`'s grillage, `C-0006`'s flatness and `C-0010`'s variance re-run on a sheet carrying `C-0055`'s upward arm array | A8.2 | **DONE** (iteration 10) — claim [`C-0061`](gpd/claims/C-0061-stacked-arm-sheet.md), raises [`CH-0074`](gpd/challenges/CH-0074-the-flat-distribution-lives-on-stations-no-placement-supplies.md). **They do EXACTLY NOTHING to the sheet's rigidity, and the reason is the same sentence that limited them to 34.** A body attached to a structure at **one** point and otherwise free has a **zero Schur complement** there — its rigid-body motions span everything one crossover can impose — so 34 arms covering **46.3 %** of the tile's plan move `C-0009`'s peak dishing, peak crossover force and peak duplex shear by `5e−9` relative, and that residual is the arm regularisation and vanishes **linearly** with it over four decades. **The single tie is forced, not chosen**: an upward site belongs to one duplex, so its pitch is the bare 32 bp = **10.88 nm**, against an arm of **8.164 nm** at 34 paths and **9.131 nm even at §3's 45**. The bracket has **three** steps and none is large — one tie adds nothing; **two ties add a TORSION BAR and nothing else** (5.832 pN·nm/rad, **0.112 %** of the dishing, because two points determine a line); the arm's own `EI` needs **three** ties, i.e. 21.76 nm of arm; the 19.3× axially coupled second layer is outside both the motif and the kinematics. **Mass: 1.21× on a quality factor of `5e−4`.** **Drag is the only channel that moves anything and it is an upper bound** — the arms are on the `+z` face, so `C-0004`'s squeeze film is untouched **by construction**, and what they add is **9.1 %** of the total drag: the nominal corner goes 91.2 → **82.9 kHz** and the **worst** §4(d) margin 22.81× → **20.73×**, still discharged. **`C-0010`'s `σ_RMS ≤ 3.0 nm` is IDENTICALLY unchanged broadband** — equipartition sees no drag and no mass — with the in-band amplitude up **4.9 %**. **What the array really changes is where the coupling ENTERS**: on `C-0055`'s own 34 roots a uniform coupling dishes **0.4156** of the stroke against 0.2182 on `C-0015`'s 3 × 15 and **0.3079** free — **1.35× worse than no coupling at all** — and `C-0058`'s flat rim rule reaches only **0.1649** there against its published 0.0753 (`CH-0074`). Two smaller results: **`C-0055`'s placement is not centro-symmetric** (centroid at `x = −8.80 nm`) and reflecting alternate rows is free and worth 0.4156 → 0.3558; and the peak crossover force is **8.3×** larger at the arm roots than on the inset grid. Opens `T-125` and `T-126`. ~~TODO — **high, and it is the largest open item `C-0055` leaves.**~~ `C-0055` moves the `E5a` array off the sheet's own crossovers and onto the unoccupied upward azimuth, which costs the host **no crossover and no duplex length** — so every table in `C-0054` (computed on *consumed* interfaces) simply does not apply, and nothing replaces them. But 34 arms of 8.16 nm are **278 nm of duplex added out of plane at one interhelical distance**, i.e. mass, drag and rigidity in a direction no model in this programme contains: `C-0009`'s grillage is a single sheet, `C-0006`'s plate is 2-D, and `C-0010`'s variance budget has no out-of-plane member. The cheap bound first — the arms' plan footprint is 0.46 of the tile and their added second moment about the sheet's mid-plane is `A d²`, which is knowable before any solve. Also unresolved: the **8 bp staple break** Ke et al. name as a yield cost has a published sign and no published size at 34 arms per tile, and the caDNAno scaffold route that visits them (35.7 % of M13 is a ledger, not a route). |
| T-122 | Can a 5:1 per-path coupling stiffness ratio be BUILT? | `C-0030`/`C-0023`'s element catalogue asked for two stiffnesses on one tile, or the statement that a coupling can only be uniform | A8.2 | **DONE** (iteration 10) — claim [`C-0060`](gpd/claims/C-0060-buildable-stiffness-ratio.md), raises [`CH-0073`](gpd/challenges/CH-0073-the-along-helix-scatter-rule-reverses-on-a-non-uniform-coupling.md) against `C-0026`'s build rule. **YES on the stiffness, NO on the placement — and the qualifier `C-0058` needs is about the ARRAY, not the ratio.** The declared falsifier — *one quantum of the design parameter taking the ratio out of the flat window* — **did not fire at any of fourteen element/level pairs**: the coarsest quantum is **19.1 %** of a level's own stiffness (an 11 bp hinge arm), the finest **1.0 %** (a 99 nt ssDNA limb), against a flat ratio window this task **measures rather than cites** at **3.5 ≤ R ≤ 20**, i.e. 471 % wide — quantisation is **25× finer than the requirement**, where `C-0023`'s mounting-offset quantum was **8.3× coarser** than its own. **The structural reason is one line**: a preload is a *length* and a stiffness is a *power* of a length, so the same 0.34 nm enters divided by 87 base pairs rather than by one. All seven settings reach both levels (realised ratios **4.667–5.144**) and **all fourteen built designs are flat**, 0.0715–0.0815 against `T-5b`'s 0.10. **A second cheap bound turned out to be load-bearing**: rounding the two *levels* independently misses `C-0017`'s mandate by **0.40–5.44 %** — a placement error, not a rounding nuisance — and **trimming individual paths by ONE base pair** (legitimate because the mandate is an equality on a **sum**) takes the worst to **`1.3e−4`** in ≤ 18 moves, at the price of 3–4 distinct staple lengths instead of two. **`T-45` is answered as a THRESHOLD**: the built design loses flatness at **34.6 %** relative scatter (31.6 % with the total held), **2.04×** `C-0026`'s 17 % break-even and **0.52×** the 66.7 % at which the two populations merely *overlap* — so **flatness binds at half the amplitude the ordering does**, and small scatter *helps*. **What fails is the array**: `k ∝ span^(−3)` makes the soft level **1.71×** the longer member, so `C-0030`'s interior span is **52.36 nm** on a 40 nm tile and places **0** of 45; `E3f` 15, `E5n1` 30, `E5n2` 15, `C39` 0 — six of seven — and only `E4` places 45, at the second ground `C-0023` already excluded it for. **A small free result: the best one-parameter ratio at `C-0058`'s own collar is 7, not 5** (0.0653 against 0.0753, a further 13.4 %), and it is no harder to build. Opens `T-127` (does a MIXED-span array pack?). ~~TODO — **high, and it is the largest open item `C-0058` leaves.**~~ `C-0058` makes the Gen-1 tile flat for the first time by giving the 34 rim stations 5× the stiffness of the 11 interior ones at `C-0017`'s unchanged total — but every distribution in it assumes an independently specifiable linear spring per station, which no claim has ever had to supply. The cheap bound is one line: `C-0030`'s flexure goes as `span^−3`, so 5× is a **1.71× span ratio** (21.44 → 12.5 nm at the same section), which `C-0041`'s packing has to admit *and* `C-0049`'s per-path allowable has to survive at the shorter, stiffer end. Also to check: `CH-0042`'s strain-softening is a **different curve per span**, so two spans do not stay in a 5:1 ratio over the stroke, and `C-0058`'s springs are linear. **If the answer is no, `CH-0071` stands only with the qualifier "for buildable couplings" and `CH-0034`'s floor returns.** |
| T-123 | Is ANY distribution flat at every one of `C-0022`'s solved states? | `C-0058`'s minimax re-run with a real optimiser, more starts and the placement freed, or a bound saying it cannot be | A8.2 | **DONE** (iteration 11) — claim [`C-0064`](gpd/claims/C-0064-robust-distribution.md), raises [`CH-0077`](gpd/challenges/CH-0077-five-solved-states-are-four-devices.md) against `C-0058`'s Deliverable 4 and `CH-0071`'s condition 2. **NO at the five states, YES over every operating RANGE — and the requirement itself was mis-posed.** A real minimax — log-sum-exp smoothing with continuation, **analytic gradients through the Woodbury solve**, conjugate gradients on the log-weights, **42 starts**, `C-0058`'s own optimiser as a polish — reaches **0.1254** of the stroke against `C-0058`'s 0.1587 (−21.0 %) and stops, still **1.25×** `T-5b`'s 0.10. **The search is not the limit**: 16 starts to 42 moves it by **exactly zero**, the homotopy is worth 8.4 %, and **two states are active at the optimum**. At a *single* state the same optimiser is **2.54× better** than `C-0058` (0.0214 against 0.0544), so every state is easy alone — the difficulty is entirely in sharing. **The obstruction is a SIGN.** Of the 31 non-empty subsets, **every one of the 14 that puts the 2 nm state with a 10 nm state fails** (0.1086–0.1254) and **every one of the other 17 is flat** (0.0090–0.0799), including everything-but-2-nm at **0.0799** and 2 nm paired with its OWN device's rest state at **0.0620**. The 2 nm state's free-tile dishing field is **anti-parallel** to every other's — cosine **−0.943 to −1.000**, exactly **−1.000** against 10 mM, where all six other pairs run **+0.949 to +0.997** — and `C-0022` says why in a row nobody had used: it is the **only one of its 21 solved states** whose finite tile carries *less* total force than a 1-D pressure over the footprint (**−3.91 %**). A distribution that flattens an edge *enhancement* deepens an edge *deficit*. **But the five states are FOUR DEVICES** — three buffers' rest states at a 10 nm layer, plus the rest and *held* states of a **5 nm** layer, of which the 2 nm one is `C-0022`'s own *"held at 3 nm stroke"* row and no state of the 10 nm device at all. Over the range each device traverses (one buffer, one layer, one bias, `L₀` → `L₀ − s`, both ends solved by `C-0022` at 0.192 V for the design device) the minimax is **0.0373** (`C-0018`'s placed 2 mM device), **0.0435** (`C-0032`'s 0.5 mM), **0.0620** (the 5 nm device) and **0.0504** (10 mM) — **all four inside `T-5b`'s 0.10**, both endpoints active at each optimum, and two interpolated intermediate gaps move the first by **exactly zero**. **A real buildability failure is reported**: the 45-parameter optimum's **quantisability is not a well-posed quantity** — its optimal set is a MANIFOLD (2 active constraints, 44 free directions), and two builds differing only in decision precision returned equally good points (0.0373 and 0.0372) whose spreads were **26.0×** and 7.9× and whose two-level projections were **0.0899** and **0.1002**, on opposite sides of `T-5b`'s line. **What answers it is that a constrained family must be searched IN, not projected ONTO**: `C-0058`'s own two-level rim × 5 rule is flat over the whole traversed range of both 10 nm devices at **0.0753** and **0.0683**, 24.9 % better than the projected optimum and already priced buildable by `C-0060`. **Cost**: 4.243 pN per path at 3 nm (2.36× clear of the 10 pN unzip allowable, inside `C-0049`'s 4.50 and *outside* its 1.35 at the desired 10 nm), 1.106 pN worst crossover (9.0× clear), 1.366 pN duplex shear, and **+91 %** on `C-0014`'s thermal force — the largest single cost of the non-uniformity. **The cheap bound did not fire**: the per-state least-squares floor is 0.00321, 31× below the tolerance, so the five-state negative is a *"not found at a large budget"* and not a theorem. **Conditional on placement**: every station is `C-0026`'s 3 × 15, and `C-0063` (`T-125`) resolves `CH-0074` on a *different* station set where `C-0058`'s rim rule REVERSES SIGN — so nothing here transfers, and **whether `C-0063`'s flat placement is flat over a RANGE is now the largest open item** (it reports 0.0706 at one state). Opens `T-129`. ~~TODO~~ **medium-high.** `C-0058` finds a distribution flat at the design point (0.0544–0.0753 of the stroke) whose worst state is 0.187, and a minimax over all five solved states that reaches only **0.1587** — marginally *above* `CH-0034`'s 0.149. That is a *"not found"*, not a *"does not exist"*: the search is a coordinate descent from three starts at fixed stations. Three things are untried — a genuine minimax formulation (the objective is a max of convex-ish surfaces and the `C-0058` surrogate makes an exchange algorithm cheap), the **placement** as a variable rather than `C-0026`'s grid, and the force-space least-squares bound evaluated **per state** to see whether a common flat point is excluded at all. **The uniform coupling is already flat at the two compressed states**, so what is wanted is a distribution that does not spoil them. |
| T-124 | Re-derive the truss branch's junctions on the torsion-feasible set | `C-0029`'s, `C-0042`'s and `C-0052`'s routings re-optimised over `C-0057`'s feasible placements, with the mechanical consequences carried through — or the statement that no feasible placement supports them | A8.2 | **DONE** (iteration 10) — claim [`C-0059`](gpd/claims/C-0059-torsion-feasible-routing.md), raises [`CH-0072`](gpd/challenges/CH-0072-the-alignment-is-not-free-on-the-torsion-feasible-set.md). **A split verdict: two of the three junctions survive on the feasible set and the third does not.** `C-0057`'s census re-derives to **departure 0** (3 546 / 1 855 and 280 / 137 of 69 120). The **single junction** closes at a chord of **90.0° — exactly the flexure axis**, `cos²ψ = 1.0000`, at a 0.643 nm binding link in the window's *interior*, 7 of the 120 best-aligned. The **pair** closes at every separation 6–12 bp but its alignment stops being free — 33°/**69°**/57°/**6°**/**6°**/33°/33° against `C-0042`'s 0.00° everywhere — so its own **7 bp row is the worst of the seven** and, at 69°, is **not representable at all** under `C-0037`'s two-axis base; **9–10 bp** delivers 6.0°. The **trio does not close**: 750 of 1 800 crossbar lattices are reach-feasible and none of the 24 best-aligned survives, 134 junction solves, 0 closing. **And `C-0029`'s recommended scaffold excursion cannot be aligned** — 1 of 120 at 39.0° against independent staples' 7 at 0.0°. **The mechanics is nearly unchanged** (1.81–2.45 / 1.36–1.84 against `C-0052`'s 1.81–2.43 / 1.36–1.83), because the binding misalignment is the leg's own quantised twist budget and not the chemistry. **The cheap bound written to bind did not** — the reach-feasible set occupies 118 of 120 azimuths — and that falsifier was declared in advance. Leaves `T-127`. |
| T-127 | Does a torsion-feasible trio exist on the crossbar at all? | `C-0059`'s trio search deepened — more lattices, more candidate azimuths per junction, the crossbar length and row pitch freed — or a bound saying the cap cannot be routed | A8.2 | **DONE** (iteration 11) — claim [`C-0062`](gpd/claims/C-0062-crossbar-trio-existence.md), raises [`CH-0075`](gpd/challenges/CH-0075-the-cap-floor-is-read-off-a-stage-that-failed.md). **THE TRIO EXISTS, at every one of the 21 admissible `(crossbar, row)` configurations, and `C-0059`'s negative was a property of its budget and not of the geometry.** The cheap bound is the whole answer: the three junctions are conditionally independent given the lattice, they close on **21.1 % / 20.0 % / 27.8 %** of lattices marginally, so `C-0059`'s **24** solved lattices were worth **0.28** trios — a null result was the expected one. Deepened to **49 857 lattices solved and 149 789 junction solves** (**1 039×** and **557×** `C-0059`'s 48 and 269, 299 578 link closures) the crossbar carries **609** closing trios: **4–13 per configuration** of 750–904 reach-feasible, **151 of 11 834** and **167 of 11 874** in the two 25 920-lattice depth runs. **Restricted to `C-0059`'s own budget this task also finds nothing**, and `C-0059`'s class still returns 750 / 24 / **134** / 0 / 6.0° at departure 0. The budget is affordable only because **`C-0057`'s reach bound is a PER-ASSIGNMENT proof of exclusion** — 12 % of the 32 assignments survive it, a **17.2×** speedup with **0 disagreements** against `bestLinkClosure`. **39 of the 44 published trios also close on `C-0057`'s 180-step verdict grid.** Two smaller results: **`C-0052`'s own 13 bp crossbar is the worst of the three lengths at its own row** (6 closures at 57° against 13 at 21° for 14 and 15 bp, and overhang is mechanically free); and **the closing count is a density on a continuum, not a converged number** — refining the phase 45 → 180 takes it 2 → 18, so at `C-0059`'s own resolution this task finds 0 too. **The mechanics does not move**: recomputed at ONE row pitch throughout and at the alignment a *closing* trio delivers, the best representable design is the **10 bp row at both ends** (base 6.0°, cap **27.0°**, 17 bp crossbar, 4.08 nm leg) at **2.45 / 1.84**, against `C-0059`'s own best 2.45 / 1.84 — **a 4.5× worse cap floor for a third-decimal change**, which is `C-0059`'s insensitivity finding tested rather than assumed. `C-0042`'s **7 bp** and `C-0037`'s **8 bp** rows stay **NOT REPRESENTABLE** (base 69.0° and 57.0°). `CH-0075`: `C-0059`'s table reads the row pitch at 7 bp for the cap and 9 bp for the base, and its cap floor is the best *reach-feasible* alignment of a stage that returned no closure. ~~TODO — **high, and it is the item that decides the truss branch.**~~ **high, and it is the item that decides the truss branch.** `C-0059` finds **0 closing trios in the 24 best-aligned of 750 reach-feasible lattices** at two candidate azimuths per junction, against a single junction that closes exactly aligned and a pair that closes at every separation. That is a *"not found within the budget"*, and the budget is thin: 24 of 750 lattices, 134 junction solves. What is untried is the crossbar **length** (only 13 and 15 bp were swept), the **row pitch** (only 7 bp, while `C-0059` moves the *sheet* row to 9–10), more azimuth candidates per junction, and the six-target distinctness as a search variable rather than a filter. **If it comes up empty at a real budget, `C-0048`'s cap has no routing and the truss branch closes** — which §7 wants early. |
| T-125 | Sweep the row phases of `C-0055`'s upward arm array | The flatness of the best 34-root placement under `C-0022`'s solved load, against `T-5b`'s 10 % and against `C-0058`'s 0.0753 | A8.2 | **DONE** (iteration 11) — claim [`C-0063`](gpd/claims/C-0063-upward-root-placement.md), **resolves [`CH-0074`](gpd/challenges/CH-0074-the-flat-distribution-lives-on-stations-no-placement-supplies.md)**, raises [`CH-0076`](gpd/challenges/CH-0076-the-mirrored-placement-is-on-the-other-face-of-the-sheet.md). **The upward array makes the Gen-1 tile FLAT, and it does it with EQUAL SPRINGS.** The best of **1 144 858** evaluated placements dishes **0.0706** of the stroke under `C-0022`'s solved load — inside `T-5b`'s 0.10, **4.36× better than no coupling at all** (0.3079), **5.9×** better than `C-0055`'s own placement (0.4156), and better than `C-0058`'s **0.0753** on a 3 × 15 grid no placement supplies — at `C-0017`'s unchanged total, with **no distribution at all**. **Two cheap bounds did the work.** The count vector is forced: `3a + 2(15 − a) = 34` gives **exactly four rows of three**. And a placement can be centro-symmetric only where `2c ≡ 0 (mod 10.88 nm)`, which is **exactly 2 of the 32 phases — 8 and 24**, both inside `C-0015`'s eight-column ten; the winner is at **24**, is centro-symmetric, and its centroid is `0.000 nm` against `C-0055`'s `−8.80`. It came from an **exhaustive** enumeration of **361 584** centro-symmetric placements, which beat the descent at its own phase (0.0706 against 0.0917). **The flat set is `C-0015`'s ten**: all ten eight-column phases reach 0.077–0.092 and only one of the other twenty-two gets inside the convention — a **fifth** independent construction landing there. **Every phase beats no coupling**, so `C-0061`'s dishing *source* is a property of its greedy scheduler, not of the lattice. **`C-0058`'s rim rule REVERSES SIGN here** — 0.0706 uniform against 0.2214 at its own ×5 — and the 34-parameter optimum wants a peak ratio of **1.30** for a further 13.9 % (0.0608), against a least-squares floor of 0.0031. **Cost unchanged in kind**: 2.30 pN in the worst path solved (2.94 pN on the mandate secant), 3.4× clear of the 10 pN unzip allowable, 0.35 pN of `C-0014` thermal force, 1.25 pN in the worst crossover (8.3× the 3 × 15's, as `C-0061` warned, still 8× clear). **`CH-0076`**: `C-0061`'s *"free, one line"* mirrored placement is **not on the upward lattice** — reflection maps a row's `EAST` sites exactly onto its `WEST` ones, so 16 of its 34 arms hang **into the grafted layer** — and its 0.4156 is read on a **phase-8 host** while the array is at phase 0, which is worth **38.9 %** (0.5771 on its own seven-column host). ~~TODO — **high, and it is the cheapest open item on the branch.**~~ `C-0061` found `C-0055`'s own placement is **not centro-symmetric** — its scheduler fills every row greedily from the low-`x` end and points every arm the same way, so the coupling centroid sits at `x = −8.80 nm` on a tile running −20 to +20 — and that reflecting the odd rows, **one line of code, free, on the same column lattice**, takes the dishing from 0.4156 to 0.3558 of the stroke and the rim × 5 design from 0.3419 to 0.2250. That is **more than `C-0058`'s whole rim rule buys on the unreflected set**. `C-0055`'s own gate-3 finding is that the per-row problems are **independent**, so the sweep is a product of per-row phases and is cheap; nobody has run it. The falsifier is that the best placement still misses `T-5b`'s 0.10, in which case `CH-0074` hardens from *"this station set is not flat"* to *"no station set the upward lattice can supply is flat"*. |

CAN share one face, and there is exactly one registration at which they do — the ties must land ON the arms.** **The section decides the plan**: the tie has to reach the **tile**, so its clear column runs from the tile's top face at `z = +1.0` to the standoff base plane and strictly contains the arm slab (**1.69–3.69 nm** at rest, **1.69–6.69 nm** swept at 3 nm), which makes every plan overlap **level-independent** — `C-0041`'s Fact A in a new place, and the reason the **0.664** area fraction settles nothing. **Every regular grid then fails**: on `C-0063`'s phase-24 placement `C-0015`'s own 3 × 15 puts **30 of 45** ties inside an arm at the published arm senses and **26** at the best of them, because **8 of the 15 rows have no choice of sense at all** (the tile edge forces every row rooted at `±16.32`); two columns give **24 of 30**, one column **10 of 15**; and **no rigid translation of a two- or three-column grid clears every row** over 400 001 offsets, the one-column grid clearing in four windows the nearest **6.785 nm off the tile centre-line**. **But the room is there** — the array leaves **108** places a tie could stand against the 45 demanded, and the poorest row holds five — **so what refuses them is REGISTRATION, not size, and `T-102` is not what this wants.** The escape (snap each tie to the feasible `x` nearest its column, worst displacement **4.332 nm**) is **nearly free and not worth taking**: it costs **+1.7 %** of dishing (0.2182 → **0.2219**) because the displacement is *along* the helices where `C-0026`'s registration is *across* them, and neither set is flat, while the design that **is** flat needs no tie grid at all (`C-0063`'s 34 roots, **0.0706**, coupling entering at the hinges). **The one free registration is the arms' own tips**: 34 ties on the arm ends clear every neighbouring arm by **2.7156 nm** against 1.345 demanded — the **root pitch minus the arm**, which as a free-standing gap clears a 2.69 nm duplex by **0.0256 nm** and fails at the 2.73 nm square-lattice value. **The sweep runs the FAVOURABLE way** (the plan projection is `√(L²−s²)`, so the swept envelope is the rest footprint identically and a static plan view is conservative), and §3's **desired 10 nm does not change the clearance — it removes the element**, corroborating `C-0050` from a plan view. ~~TODO — **medium-high, and it is the one thing `C-0061` states without solving.**~~ `C-0035` found exactly one buildable mounting: standoff bases on the **output superstructure**, standoffs pointing **away** from the tile, the flexure **outboard** of its own ground, and each midspan tied back **down** through that ground to the tile. `C-0055`'s upward arms occupy a slab **1.69–3.69 nm above the sheet surface over 46.3 % of the plan**, on the same `+z` side. `C-0061` shows the arms cost the actuation gap nothing — they are on the dry face, and every number in `C-0035`'s occupancy table is untouched — but the tie-down path crosses exactly the space they occupy, and `C-0035`'s own note that *"the tile now carries no out-of-plane element at all"* is no longer true of a design that adopts the escape. `CH-0055`'s 326 nm² tie aperture and its **18-component** severance verdict are the other half of the same geometry. |

| T-131 | **Reconcile `ANSWERS.md` against the claims, line by line** | Every number and verdict in `ANSWERS.md` traced to a standing claim, with each superseded statement corrected or explicitly marked superseded — or a list of the statements that cannot be traced | — | **DONE** (iteration 12) — claim [`C-0067`](gpd/claims/C-0067-answers-reconciliation.md), **raising no challenge**. **84 statements adjudicated: 63 TRACE, 16 had DRIFTED, 2 are UNTRACEABLE, 4 completeness gaps closed.** The arithmetic is near-perfect — **414 of 415** numeric tokens appear in the corpus and the one that does not is `42.4`, a rounding of `C-0032`'s `42.38` — so **the drift is in the STATUS of answers, not in the value of numbers**. Worst kind, three instances: entries of *"What we cannot answer"* that the programme had **answered** and left standing for up to **seven iterations** (`T-60`→`C-0033`, `T-75`/`T-78`→`C-0035`, `T-76`→`C-0032`/`C-0049`) — **a deliverable that under-claims is as wrong as one that over-claims and is harder to catch**, because a reviewer checks assertions and not disclaimers.
