Tooling: [`tools/trace-answers.py`](tools/trace-answers.py) + **22 checks** in [`tools/test-trace-answers.py`](tools/test-trace-answers.py), retained so the next pass costs one command. **Left undone: the window is still unsynthesised against iterations 8–11** (`C-0052`–`C-0064`, `CH-0065`–`CH-0077`) — recorded in `ANSWERS.md` as a coverage statement rather than assumed away. |
| T-130 | Do `C-0062`'s closing trio lattices PLACE 34 times? | The closing lattices carried into `C-0053`'s plan view, or the statement that a trio which closes in isolation cannot be repeated | A8.2 | **DONE** (iteration 12) — claim [`C-0065`](gpd/claims/C-0065-crossbar-array-placement.md), raises [`CH-0078`](gpd/challenges/CH-0078-the-base-floor-is-a-minimum-over-a-coordinate-the-array-pins.md). **YES — all 44 of `C-0062`'s recorded trios place 34 times, and the plan view is not what the composition costs.** Three cheap bounds settle the plan first: 34 truss blocks cover **0.385** of the footprint, the widest crossbar demands **9.49 nm** against the **10.88 nm** station pitch (`C-0053`'s 45-path arm demands 11.82 and does *not* clear it), and — **the bound that fixes the shape of the answer** — every one of `C-0063`'s 34 stations is the **SAME helical phase of its OWN host duplex**, 24 bp from that duplex's `NORTH` plane, because adjacent rows are phase-shifted by exactly the 16 bp their sites are offset by. **So the register is ONE question, not thirty-four, and the count is quantised at 0 or 34.** It comes out 34: 0 overlaps, 0 leg clashes, one level, at every trio. **What the composition finds is a REGISTER.** A leg's *base* sits only where the host's backbone offers one — **17 of 89** axial positions do — and **no row pitch closes at the station itself**, so the array translates **0.17 nm (9 bp) to 3.91 nm (6 bp)**, and **no crossover phase can absorb it** because the phase moves the station and the backbone together (a gate test). Pinning that coordinate re-reads the floor `C-0059` measured and `C-0062` built its table on: the **10 bp** row — `C-0062`'s own recommendation — reads **57.0°** against its published **6.0°** (**9.5×**), past the **45°** at which `C-0037`'s `TwoLinkBase` cannot represent a base at all (`CH-0078`); the 6.0° exists 2.55 nm away, at the price of the rim and the flatness. **The row an array can build is 9 bp at 18.0°**, and it keeps the tile flat — **0.0780** of the stroke against `C-0063`'s 0.0706 at 34 nominal stations, inside `T-5b`'s 0.10, with splitting one station into two leg bases costing 6.1 % and the register a further 4.2 %. **17 of the 44 trios survive every clause at once** (place 34, close on the verdict grid, representable base, flat tile) — the 9, 11 and 12 bp rows. **The flexure is the other story and is reported apart**: with `C-0030`'s 27.41 nm span the same array covers **1.84×** the footprint, needs **7 levels** and places **12 of 34** — `C-0041`'s standing obstruction, independent of every trio. Also recorded: this task's prompt quoted *"93 of 5 940 … at 3.00°"*, numbers that appear nowhere in `C-0062` and that `JOURNAL.md` already records as retracted first-report text. Opens `T-132`, `T-133`. ~~TODO~~ |
| T-132 | Does the leg's own length budget survive the PINNED base misalignment? | `C-0052`'s `chordPairMisalignment(m)` re-composed against `C-0065`'s registered base misalignment at one global leg length, over the whole 12–26 step envelope — or the statement that no leg length serves all 34 instances | A8.2 | **DONE** (iteration 13) — claim [`C-0070`](gpd/claims/C-0070-pinned-leg-budget.md), raises [`CH-0082`](gpd/challenges/CH-0082-a-truss-has-two-legs-and-the-design-table-gives-it-one-base.md), discharges [`CH-0078`](gpd/challenges/CH-0078-the-base-floor-is-a-minimum-over-a-coordinate-the-array-pins.md)'s item 2 and **upholds it**. **YES — the budget survives, at one leg length for all 34 instances, and the shared-length clause costs NOTHING.** Cheap bound 1 decides the shape: `C-0065`'s phase-class congruence carries from the station to the **68 leg bases**, which fall into exactly **2 classes of 34** presenting **1** distinct `(low leg, high leg)` pair at every row pitch — so *"one length for 34 instances"* collapses to *"one length for two legs"*, and the declared falsifier did not fire. What pinning actually does is make the base **OVERSPEND** `C-0052`'s budget rather than spend it exactly: a free rotation always opposes the budget's sense and a pinned one need not, so `ψ_base + ψ_cap ≥ \|m τ − 90°\|` **always**, and at the recommended design the two ends spend **81.13°** of a **45.13°** budget (**1.80×**). **And nothing fails** — at the 9 bp row **all 15** leg lengths of the 12–26 envelope are representable and pass all nine predicates, at margins **1.815–2.443** (CanDo) and **1.364–1.836** (Fields). The best shared length is **12 steps, 4.08 nm**, at **every** surviving row; at 9 bp it carries **2.443 / 1.836**, *better* than `C-0062`'s own 2.410 / 1.812 there (a worse cap moves couple into the plane that governs) and **0.13 % below** the 2.446 of the 10 bp row the array cannot build. **`C-0065`'s 17 of 44 and its 9 bp / 18.0° recommendation are unchanged** — the tightening is zero. One new constraint appears and does not bind: a truss has **two** legs pinned at **two** azimuths (9.0° and 18.0° at 9 bp), so their cap chords differ by that angle at every length, giving a floor `\|fold(δ_A−δ_B)\|/2` — 4.5° here, but **28.5°** at the 6 and 10 bp rows against `C-0062`'s composed 9.0° and 27.0° (`CH-0082`). One sensitivity moves the verdict: a **0.5 nm lateral seat** leaves the 9 bp row not representable at any leg length. ~~TODO~~ |
| T-133 | Is there an output element that does not lie in the plan? | An element whose 34 instances place at one level on `C-0063`'s stations, or the statement that the truss branch's output stage is refused by the plan view at every span | A8.2 | **DONE** (iteration 13) — claim [`C-0069`](gpd/claims/C-0069-output-element-placement.md), raises [`CH-0081`](gpd/challenges/CH-0081-a-rigid-root-demands-a-longer-arm-than-the-plan-admits.md) against the truss branch's output stage. **NO element out of the plan exists, and none is needed — the plan was never what refused the flexure.** Of an **11-row catalogue**, **3 place** all 34 at one level and **2 survive every clause**: `C-0055`/`C-0063`'s own **hinge-rooted arm** (**8.16439 nm**, 34 of 34, one level, 0.463 of the footprint, tangent minimum **30.03 pN/nm** over `[0, 3]` clearing **6 of `C-0017`'s six** 2 mM floors, 2.941 pN per path, two-sided) and a **standoff-headed crank** (`C-0028`'s `B2` base + a pinned tip, **5.331 nm**, 1.53× shorter, but only **4 of 6** floors, a compression member at 2.45× of Euler margin, and a second undemonstrated motif). **Three closed-form bounds settle the element space before any packer runs.** The plan budget on **every** 34-root placement is `pitch − d` = **8.19 nm** exactly (four rows of three are forced by `3a + 2(15−a) = 34`; the bisected ceiling agrees to `<1e−9`). The two-support flexure family's **shortest possible** member is `(48 EI/k)^(1/3)` = **22.41 nm — 2.74×** the budget, so it is refused **at every span, every end joint and every placement**, which is strictly stronger than `C-0065`'s 12 of 34. And the normal direction supplies only **axial** stiffness: `S/k` = **1122 nm**, 112× `C-0017`'s whole envelope. **What refuses the flexure is its END CONDITION**: `c` is 48–192 supported twice and ≤ 12 supported once, and the span is `c^(1/3)`. The other escapes fail too — a **fold** shrinks the budget (28 of 34 at the contour where the straight element places 34), a second **level** is reached only by a vertical member whose clash is level-independent (`C-0041`), and a **single strand** is compact (6.04 nm) and places and is **one-sided**, measured at negative argument. **The budget reduces to one inequality, `c ≤ 2.3416`, and both of the arm's joints sit inside it by 2–3 %**: at a one-crossover root the tip may be no stiffer than **79.68** and `C-0034`'s `A2` is **78.24**; at an `A2` tip the root may be no stiffer than **13.93** and one crossover is **13.53**. Neither joint was chosen for that reason and **the design has no margin on either**. The window has a **floor** as well, `1.5×` the stroke = 4.50 nm — a pinned tip at a one-crossover root asks for 3.591 nm and is **refused by its own kinematics, short enough to place and too short to work**. **Three sensitivities close the 0.0256 nm margin**: the 2.73 nm square-lattice interhelical distance (18 of 34), the top of Chen et al.'s own fitted `α` bracket (30 of 34), and §3's 45 paths (24 of 34) — so **34 is the count the plan budget prefers**, not a rounding of 45. `C-0065`'s 27.4119472 nm span and its **12 of 34** both reproduce at departure `0.0`. ~~TODO~~ |
| T-134 | **Does a tolerance model erase the branch's two knife edges?** | A scatter model for the plan positions, applied to `C-0069`'s 0.0256 nm arm margin and `C-0066`'s 0.0256 nm tie clearance, with the placed count as a distribution rather than a point — or the statement that no defensible scatter model exists and both margins are unquotable | A8.2 | **DONE** (iteration 14) — claim [`C-0072`](gpd/claims/C-0072-plan-tolerance-model.md), raises [`CH-0084`](gpd/challenges/CH-0084-the-measured-staple-incorporation-is-past-the-flatness-threshold.md). **YES, and it does not need a distribution to do it — but a distribution turned up anyway.** **The two knife edges are ONE lattice quantity**, `M = p − d − L`: `C-0069` groups it `(p − d) − L` and `C-0066` groups it `(p − L) − d`, the two agree to **`4.4e−16` nm** at every lattice constant tested, and neither claim noticed. **Four floors exceed it, none of which needs a fabrication measurement**: the base-pair **rise** (**13.28×** — so the margin is below the finest length any DNA design can *specify*, and no correction can recover it), the disagreement between the two published SAXS interhelical **means** (1.56×), the **thermal axial** breathing of the two segments the margin differences (10.46×), and the arm tip's own bending at a **perfectly rigid** root (**70.6×**, so no joint stiffening escapes). The one channel that falls *inside* rests on `C-0009`'s **constructed** in-plane crossover spring at the top of its own four-decade sweep (0.87×) — which is why the floors were required to rest on measured constants. **Correlation is worth exactly 7×**: the rise enters through the host's **32 bp** pitch and the element's **24 bp** length, so common mode carries the **difference** (threshold **1.10 %**) and opposed the **sum** (**0.158 %**), independent RMS between; the null is at a 4:3 differential strain and **no build can reach it**. **The twist propagates with coefficient exactly zero** — 10.5 and 10.67 bp/turn both round to a 32 bp interface. **And the literature is not a negative.** 77 recorded queries found the measurement in supplementary material nobody quotes: Fischer et al. (2016) fit the **single-layer sheet's** lattice-constant width at **2.5 Å on 27.41 Å — 9.1 %, 9.76× the margin and 8.27× the loosest threshold here** (Lorentzian `B/q₀` = 15.2 % beside it as an upper bound), with the multilayer brick **3.1× better ordered** — and Kube et al. (2020) could not solve a single-layer rectangle at all, *"due to excessive conformational heterogeneity"*. **The framing changes too**: Bai et al. (2012) measure the interhelical distance as a **deterministic sawtooth, 18.5 Å at a crossover to 36 Å midway**, so 2.69 nm is a **Bragg lattice constant**; read through the plan model its ends give **+0.866** and **−0.884 nm**, and its minimum is *inside* the 2.0 nm steric diameter this project asserts. **`T-45` is answered from published measurement**: Strauss et al. (2018) map incorporation over all 168 staples of a Rothemund rectangle at **48–95 %, mean 84 %** — as a per-path dropout, **43.6 %** relative scatter, **1.26×** `C-0060`'s flatness threshold and **2.57×** `C-0026`'s break-even, with a **16 %** mandate shortfall needing no pattern assumption (`CH-0084`). **`C-0070`'s seat is not a tolerance axis at all** — the verdict is **not monotone**, 4 of 11 seats pass and the failures alternate; it is a register. **The design that recovers the margin is a reduced path count** — 30 paths dissolve `C-0063`'s four forced rows of three, ceiling 8.19 → **9.12 nm**, margin **0.0256 → 1.3495 nm (53×)** — **and it loses `T-5b`'s flatness**, 0.0706 → 0.2603, so the margin and the flatness are bought from the same four arms. Opens `T-136`, `T-137`, `T-138`. ~~TODO~~ |

| T-148 | **`CH-0084`'s flatness half, under a POSITION-DEPENDENT dropout** | `C-0060`'s flatness pipeline re-run with staple incorporation as a Bernoulli dropout that is worst at the edges (Strauss et al.: 48 % edge, 95 % centre, 84 % mean), against `T-5b`'s 0.10 — or the statement that the spatial structure changes the verdict and by how much | A1.2 | **DONE** (iteration 17) — claim [`C-0087`](gpd/claims/C-0087-position-dependent-staple-dropout.md), raises [`CH-0102`](gpd/challenges/CH-0102-the-forty-eight-per-cent-is-one-corner-of-one-hundred-and-sixty-eight.md) against `C-0072`/`CH-0084`'s EDGE reading. **NEGATIVE AND UNANIMOUS, and the position dependence is not what does it.** Over the twenty `placement × convention` cells read as built the LOWEST exceedance of `T-5b`'s 0.10 anywhere is **90.6 %** of realisations and **17 of 20** exceed it in more than 98 % — at five incorporation conventions, three mandate readings and all four standing placements (`C-0017`'s 45 equal, `C-0058`'s two-level, `C-0063`/`C-0068`'s 34 equal roots, `C-0074`'s recommended 30). **The cheap bound explains it in `n` solves**: removing **exactly one** of `C-0063`'s 34 paths takes 0.0706 → **0.5010**, and one of `C-0058`'s 45 takes 0.0753 → **0.3060** — an exhaustively optimised placement is a cancellation and a cancellation has no tolerance to a missing term. `CLAUDE.md`'s *"an attachment coupling can be a NET DISHING SOURCE"* is the mechanism: a dropout IS an increase in the attachment pitch. **The declared falsifier F1 did not fire** — the four position-dependent conventions agree; the position dependence is worth **1.2–1.7×** on the 90th percentile, real and adverse, on a quantity a count of one has already taken 4–7× past the tolerance. **The ranking of the two flat designs REVERSES**: `C-0063`'s 34 equal springs beat `C-0058`'s two-level 45 at zero dropout (0.0706 / 0.0753) and lose under the measured dropout (0.6391 / 0.4893 at p90, 99.8 % / 97.0 %), because the denser array loses less per missing path — the equal-spring advantage is a fabrication LIABILITY. Neither holding the mandate (0.6719) nor compensating by `1/p` (0.6454) repairs it. **The measurement itself was read further**: the 168-value per-staple map of Suppl. | T-162 | **Does a coupling that is NOT an array escape the count argument?** | The 90th-percentile dishing under `C-0087`'s measured dropout of a coupling in which the tile is tied to one **stiff shared body** at many points, against `T-5b`'s 0.10 — or the statement that the shared body cannot be built or does not change the count | A8.2 | **DONE** (iteration 19) — claim [`C-0093`](gpd/claims/C-0093-shared-body-coupling.md), raises [`CH-0108`](gpd/challenges/CH-0108-the-flexure-bound-is-a-bound-on-a-path-count-not-on-a-family.md) and [`CH-0109`](gpd/challenges/CH-0109-a-cancellation-and-a-robust-design-are-different-designs-only-within-one-topology.md). **NO — and the negative is a much narrower one than `C-0089`'s.** A shared body is not a rescaling of an array: it is the same Woodbury system with **one added term**, `(T⁻¹ + M + F_b) f = w_free`, and the array is its `F_b = 0` corner — so `InfluenceSurrogate.solveWithDropout` now delegates to `solveWithSharedBody` and `C-0063`'s 0.0706145537 reproduces to **2.9e−10**. **The declared falsifier `F1` did NOT fire**: a rigid shared body at `C-0063`'s own 34 stations dishes **0.0344013403** at zero defects against the array's 0.0706145537, **2.05× flatter on the identical stations** and `T-5b`-flat at every rung of the tie ladder. Under the measured dropout the best of **39 graded cells** is **0.24028028** at the 90th percentile (a 6 × 15 grid of 90 rigid ties) against the array's 0.532748246 on the same stations (**2.22×**) and `C-0089`'s best-of-22 **0.284537599** (**1.18×**) — the lowest this programme has reached — with the **exceedance falling 100 % → 76.60 %** where `C-0089`'s lowest anywhere was 97.16 %. **The mechanism is a division, not a mechanics**: `C-0017`'s mandate is a SUM over an array's paths (0.980392157 pN/nm each at 34), and under a shared body the same equality is `series(Σtᵢ, g)` — the compliance moves into the body's GROUND, **99.90 %** of it at the stiffest ties, which is a rigid-body mode of the tile and therefore invisible to dishing — so the tie is capped by `C-0049`'s per-path FORCE instead, 3.33333333 pN/nm, **3.4×** more local support at 34 paths and **9.0×** at 90. **What still refuses it is the count, now MEASURED on seven grids rather than derived on one**: the shared body's redundancy slope is **−0.784357442** against the array's **−0.264642174**, **2.96× steeper**, and the log-log fit crosses 0.10 at **252.126899 ties** (an extrapolation only 1.40× beyond the densest grid measured) against the **53** upward crossover sites `C-0066` counts at phase 24 — **4.8× short**, where `C-0089`'s array is 5.7× short of 34. **And the body's own rigidity is a first-order variable**: a four-layer honeycomb brick is 1.564× the rigid body's condensed station compliance and reads **0.100166871** at 34 stations, *worse than the array*; only the rigid limit wins there. The closed form ran first and reproduces three `CLAUDE.md` entries at once — a FREE rigid body's condensation is `T − TA(AᵀTA)⁻¹AᵀT`, rank `n − 3`, **exactly zero at 1, 2 and 3 ties** and annihilating every affine tile motion at four. **`F2` FIRED and was informative**: the winner amplifies **20.96×** under one missing tie against the array's 2.06–7.10 — because an amplification is a RATIO and its absolute worst single removal is 0.139210902 against 0.377754016, **2.71× better** (`CH-0109`). **`F5` fired**: 3 of 7 buildability rows do not clear, and the cleanest is a SPECIFICATION gap — a body tied at many out-of-plane sites **is** square-lattice multilayer origami (`C-0055`, Ke et al. 2009, the one motif here with a published precedent), and §3 names a single-layer tile. `C-0041`'s severance does not transfer (a crossover is a tie, not a hole) and `CH-0081`'s 22.414 nm turns out to be `(48EI/k)^(1/3)` **at 34 paths**, 4.36–7.92 nm when the mandate is massed on one to six ground elements (`CH-0108`). | T-159 | **Repair the doubling force ladder, and reprice `CH-0099` at the other 96 states** | `TwoSpringElastica.forceForDisplacement` continuing rather than doubling, with `C-0084`'s 108 fold rows re-read at the corrected element domain — or the statement that only the recommended device's 12 matter | A8.2 | **DONE** (iteration 19) — claim [`C-0096`](gpd/claims/C-0096-doubling-ladder-repair.md), task [`T-159`](gpd/tasks/T-159.md), result `gpd/results/T-159-doubling-ladder-repair.json`, diff `gpd/data/T-159-downstream-diff.json`, raises [`CH-0112`](gpd/challenges/CH-0112-a-lost-branch-is-recorded-as-a-fold-and-as-a-ceiling.md), **resolves `C-0092`'s open items 3 and 4 and discharges `CH-0099` in its consequence**. **ONLY SIX OF THE 96 COULD EVER HAVE MOVED, AND THE REPAIR FOUND TWO MORE COPIES OF THE DEFECT.** The cheap bound is a containment, not a tolerance: `strokeCeiling = min(layer, element)` and correcting the element domain only raises the second argument, so of the 108 rows **54** carry no elastica and **36** are layer-owned (4.33–6.50 nm) — 90 excluded from one pass over a file — **18** are element-owned, `C-0092` re-read 12, and the **6** outstanding are 10 nm / 10 mM, which have **no operating point at all**. The repair: `forceForDisplacement` **continues** the branch, anchoring each force step's shooting root on the previous accepted one, **shrinking** its step instead of doubling on when a branch test fails, and **refusing** where no root below `π/2` exists — **8.1404072 nm** against the ladder's 7.91968584 (`max_s\|φ\| = 1.5688653` rad, 0.9988 of a right angle, inside the 8.16439083 contour), and it is **cheaper**: 190 RK4 sweeps against 209. All 108 rows were re-run anyway and **0 of the 90 excluded moved** a ceiling, an owner, a binding ceiling, a margin, a fold or a verdict. The *"element model branch end"* now binds at **0 of 108** — `CH-0099`'s candidate stands, its **2.567–3.740×** price is withdrawn — and the 10 nm bands become **1.3877–7.3137** / **1.8706–10.9072** with **the minima unchanged to the last digit**. `C-0033`'s collar argument is **filed** and its premise checked: **80 of 80** measured `d ln μ/dh` positive, over 2.00–11.0 nm against a 1.8696 nm ceiling gap. **`F3` FIRED, bounded**, and that is the find: the lost branch was recorded in **three** vocabularies — a *fold* (`C-0039`, 2 of 34 placements), a *ceiling* (`C-0084`) and a *note* (`C-0050`, 2 rows) — and a **fourth** record moved for a different reason, `C-0046`'s binding lists being decided by a **tie** at the 10 pN allowable while no emitted number of either row moves. **No verdict moved anywhere.** `C-0039`'s ladder is retained as an opt-in `BranchStrategy.DOUBLING_LADDER` so `C-0092`'s measurement stays a measurement — without it `T-157` moves 53 of 1053 fields. Raises `T-164`, `T-165`. |

½kΔφ² − kθ₀Δφ + const` leaves the stiffness matrix untouched, so a prestrain is a **load**, the field is linear in `θ₀`, and the triangle inequality puts `T-5b`'s 0.10 at **15.45°** of uniform row-end parameter in a way `C-0099`'s stiffness sweep was not. **The falsifier the task was proposed with is FALSE and that is a result**: a uniform prestrain is an **eigenstrain**, and the state that relaxes angular deviation"*, which is the dihedral coordinate `C-0009` already carries. | T-182 | **What prestrain does a row-end crossover actually carry?** | An oxDNA or all-atom edge-crossover equilibration giving the residual dihedral angle between two duplexes joined by a raster turn at a duplex terminus, against `T-172`'s 15.45° threshold — or the statement that the quantity is not extractable from such a simulation | A1.2, with A8.2 | **DONE** (iteration 22) — claim [`C-0107`](gpd/claims/C-0107-row-end-prestrain-value.md), task [`T-182`](gpd/tasks/T-182.md), result `gpd/results/T-182-row-end-prestrain-value.json`, raises [`CH-0122`](gpd/challenges/CH-0122-the-prestrain-threshold-is-a-secant-not-a-triangle-inequality.md). **THE CHEAP BOUND DOES NOT CLOSE IT AND `C-0099`'S RECOMMENDATION IS REVERSED.** All **8** ceilings lie **above** `C-0104`'s 15.4497275 deg: rupture by 5.5-36x (the couple at the threshold is **3.65 pN nm**, 1.82 pN per bond), the register quantum at 17.143, Rothemund's own *"one or two scaffold bases"* remedy at 34.29-68.57. **`C-0104`'s ladder is the wrong ladder**: its rungs are PER-DOMAIN offsets, but every domain's error carries the same sign (Rothemund: *"the helical domains between crossovers are slightly overtwisted or undertwisted"*), so it ACCUMULATES and the duplex's own torsion is what stops it. The boundary layer `u(+-L/2) = dw x lambda x tanh(L/2 lambda)`, `lambda = sqrt(C p/k_theta) = 11.8-24.0 nm` against a **19.04 nm** half-row, leaves **17.15-24.98 deg** over a 12-cell bracket — **12 of 12 above the threshold**, against an un-relieved 30.00. **The SIGN composes to the ADVERSE map**: the glide symmetry's `(-1)^b` and the raster's alternating ends cancel, giving `C-0104`'s **uniform** distribution, the only one of its three that crosses 0.10. Read on the 14 row-end sites alone the published placement **fails in both signs** (0.1022820 / 0.1193334); read as the **field** it is — `(-1)^b u(x)` at all 56 — it **holds** at **0.0922622**, with **7.7 %** of the convention unused where zero prestrain leaves 37.9 %. **The one published measurement of this exact coordinate excludes exactly these sites**: Snodin et al. (2019, already in `gpd/data/T-151-sources/`) measure the interduplex corrugation of a 2D origami by oxDNA at ~8-9 deg average and ~+-16 deg per junction and write *"we ... exclude the outermost junctions on the tile"* — and their tile is **twist-corrected** (31/32 bp mix) where `C-0086`'s 112 bp raster is not. **So the oxDNA run is WARRANTED and it fits this box**: one row end plus two neighbours, **~256 nt** against `T-9`'s own *"2-5 k nucleotides ... days not weeks"*, protocol published, no GPU. 8 ceilings, 12 boundary-layer cells, 11 solved states, 8 literature records, 5 convergence records, 7 reproductions, 6 falsifiers; **16 gate-named tests**; `C-0090`'s **0.0621469105** reproduced at **3.0e-08** on an INDEPENDENT explicit-elastic-support path (no influence bank, so `CH-0120`'s trap cannot fire); result file re-run through `tools/study.sh` and **byte-identical**; `tools/verify.sh` **BUILD SUCCESSFUL in 21 m 34 s**, no test failure, with only a sibling's mid-TDD `coupling/CountPhaseInteractionStudy.kt` dropped. **`F6` fired once and was right to**: the standing uniform-load falsifier read 0.170 on the 34-anchor coupled lattice — a sparse coupling IS a dishing source (`C-0060`) — and reads **2.126e-07** where it belongs, on the support-free lattice. | T-189 | **Can `C-0086`'s 112 bp raster row be TWIST-CORRECTED?** | A row length (or a mix of inter-junction domain lengths) that keeps `C-0086`'s seamless boustrophedon and takes the design twist to B-DNA's, with the `C-0090` placement re-read under the reduced boundary layer — or the statement that the connectivity quantisation and the twist correction are incompatible | A8.2 | **DONE** (iteration 31) — claim [`C-0133`](gpd/claims/C-0133-twist-corrected-raster-row.md), task [`T-189`](gpd/tasks/T-189-twist-corrected-raster.md), result `gpd/results/T-189-twist-corrected-raster.json`, model `src/main/kotlin/structure/TwistCorrectedRaster.kt`, study `structure.TwistCorrectedRasterStudyKt`. **BOTH BRANCHES OF THE ACCEPTANCE ARE DELIVERED.** The two quantisations are **exactly incompatible** and it is one line: `N = 180q/34.2857 = 21q/4` with `q` odd, and `21q` is odd, so **no integer row length is ever exactly an odd number of half turns at B-DNA's twist** — at any width, at any domain mix. **But the residual is an INVARIANT**: exactly **0.25 bp = 8.5714°** of accumulated twist across the whole tile, at every admissible width, against `C-0086`'s 112 bp row's **1.75 bp = 60.0°**, exactly **7×**. The construction near Sec 3's 40 nm exists and it is **110 bp = 37.40 nm**, `16+15+16+16+16+15+16`, whose per-interface spacings are `31,31,32,32,31,31` — **Snodin's measured 31/32 mix, from arithmetic**. Re-optimised the corrected tile is genuinely better — **0.0580196384** against **0.0817325013** — and the **design moves**. Costs **one base pair of arm** (24 → 23 rises) and the station lattice's centro-symmetry unless the out-of-plane offset is mirrored (**30.0°** of azimuth at 8 of 52 stations). Raises [`CH-0158`](gpd/challenges/CH-0158-the-admissible-width-list-assumes-an-uncorrected-twist.md) and [`CH-0159`](gpd/challenges/CH-0159-a-zero-plan-clearance-cannot-survive-a-width-change.md). Spawns `T-216`, `T-217`. |
| T-190 | **What do the 42 INTERIOR crossovers carry, and does their cancellation hold?** | The graded corrugated field `(-1)^b u(x)` re-read over the interior sites separately from the 14 row-end ones, with the cancellation decomposed | A8.2 | **DONE** (iteration 23) — [`C-0112`](gpd/claims/C-0112-interior-crossover-prestrain.md). **The field is exactly separable and the verdict is not.** A prestrain is a load, so the split into 14 + 42 is an IDENTITY (**2.1e-15** in the coefficient vector); peak dishing is a **seminorm** and does not add (the graded peak is **0.294** of the sum of its parts'), so the cancellation is a **cross term**, `cos = -0.579495374`. **The 42 interior sites are the larger half** — **53.65 %** of the assembled absolute couple, `\|I\|/\|R\| = 0.688` — and the cancellation is structural and needs no solve: the column next inboard carries **0.661** of the row end's amplitude at the **opposite** sign, and one column pair recovers **106.2 %** of the whole move. **`C-0107`'s 0.0922622 SURVIVES**, reproduced at **1.1e-10**, flat at **both** overall signs (0.0922622 / **0.0910197**) and at **40 of 40** bracket cells against **14 of 40** for the row-end-only idealisation. | T-152 | **What stacking-prevention clearance should the collinear slot carry, and does the whole branch's plan budget move?** | A collinear clearance quantised at the 0.34 nm rise, with the `Q5` margin, the `c ≤ 2.3416` joint window and `C-0053`'s 43-of-45 all re-read at it — or the statement that the allowance is a fabrication number this programme cannot set | A8.2 | **DONE** (iteration 17) — claim [`C-0085`](gpd/claims/C-0085-collinear-stacking-clearance.md), task [`T-152`](gpd/tasks/T-152.md), raises [`CH-0100`](gpd/challenges/CH-0100-the-collinear-margin-is-an-integer-not-a-residue.md). **SIX BASE PAIRS, 2.04 nm — and the cheap bound reshaped the question before any code ran: the MARGIN IS AN INTEGER, not a residue.** A collinear gap is an **axial** length between two duplex end faces, so it is quantised at the rise like the other two terms, and `M = (32 − N_d − N_L)` rises. The published **0.02561 nm** is what is left when a **transverse** SAXS constant (**7.912** rises) and an elastica root (**24.013** rises) are subtracted from an integer pitch; on the lattice the standing design closes at **exactly zero**, and quantising the convention *up* to the 8 rises a design can draw makes the margin **negative** and drops the array to **30 of 34**. **The criterion is an energy and the geometry supplies it**: both faces are tethered to the same sheet, so a stack costs the elastic work of closing the gap, and holding the stacked state below one per cent on the softest closure path (30.518 pN/nm) asks **1.90518 nm**. The adopted ladder spans **4/5/5/6** rises across a 2× closure stiffness and a 100× occupancy, so the whole modelling exposure is two rises and **the design as built already carries 8**. Margin **0.67561 nm = 2 WHOLE RISES, 26.38×** the published one. **All three of `C-0071`'s live `NONE` bands become real margins** — the plan length at 2 whole rises, and: tip 79.678 → **133.687** (**1.7088×** `A2`, from 1.0184×), root 13.930 → **25.689** (**1.8988×** one crossover, from 1.0296×), `c ≤ 2.34166` → **2.94462**. Nothing moves adversely — 34 of 34 at one level, `C-0053`'s 45-arm count **43 → 43**, `C-0074`'s 30-root ceiling **9.535 → 9.86 nm** — and `C-0069`'s midspan-family negative survives a clearance of **zero** (22.414 nm against a bare 10.88 pitch). **Two surprises.** The **conservatism is load-bearing**: a rigid-rooted arm (9.247 nm) *places* at 4 rises or fewer, so the loose end of the stacking range would reopen the truss branch `CH-0081` closed. And **distance is the only control the design still owns** — all three of Rothemund's measured anti-stacking remedies need a strand **terminus**, and `C-0034`'s `A2` has spent both of the two a duplex end has (`C-0029`'s counting theorem, striking twice on the same links). No falsifier fired. |
| T-140 | Does the Rothemund SEAM break `C-0076`'s node congruence? | A weave phase model carrying the scaffold seam, checked against `C-0063`'s 34 stations — or the statement that no station lies within one weave period of a seam | A8.2 | **DONE** (iteration 16) — claim [`C-0081`](gpd/claims/C-0081-seam-weave-congruence.md). **YES, wherever it sits, and the acceptance's second branch is answered NO.** A seam does not perturb the weave, it **deletes an extremum**: at any even plane every duplex participates in exactly one interface crossover, so removing the junctions at plane `s` removes exactly **one pull event from every duplex**, every duplex is straight at `±Δ/4` from `s−2` to `s+2`, and every interface is pinned at `mean ± Δ/2` instead of passing through its mean at the odd planes. The 40 nm tile is **3.68 weave periods** wide and admits **8** seam planes; every one of them puts **6–12 of the 34 stations** inside its straight window (**12–20** within a full period), so **22–28 of 34** survive on a node against 34 with no seam. There is nowhere for a seam to hide. **`C-0076`'s VERDICT still stands, on its other argument** — `M = p − d − L` is an along-helix identity between unbonded bodies and carries no weave coordinate, so the plan margin is **0.898333453 nm at all 8 positions, one distinct value**. `CLAUDE.md`'s *a verdict that survives can survive on a different reason*, and the first instance where the surviving reason was named in advance by the claim being qualified. **What the seam costs is `C-0076`'s headline**: the departure at an affected station is exactly `Δ/2`, unit slope, so the annihilated **1.2–1.75 nm amplitude bracket is restored at full strength** — 0.600 / 0.750 / 0.875 nm at Yoo / Snodin / Bai. **And the sign is the opposite of the wording**: *opens up* is the favourable half; the other parity **closes** by the same amount, so the worst across-row clearance at a station falls 0.872724 → **0.122724** nm at Snodin's amplitude and to **−0.002276 nm** at Bai's — a clash, at the measured phosphate girth, which `T-139` can only make worse. **The model reproduces Snodin's own sentence without being told to**: one group of pairs at the maximum and *the other … opens up much less* at the minimum, from the shared duplexes' geometry alone, with no appeal to the *extra scaffold crossovers* he attributes it to — the only independent check the model has. **The remedy is free and is an INPUT**: this programme's tile has no scaffold routing, and a seamless one restores `C-0076` in full. Raises no challenge — `C-0076` named this counter-case itself. Opens `T-151`. |
| T-151 | **Can the Gen-1 tile be raster-folded WITHOUT a scaffold seam?** | A scaffold routing for a 15-duplex 40 nm single-layer sheet with no seam, or the statement that one is unavailable at this aspect ratio | A8.2 | **DONE** (iteration 17) — claim [`C-0086`](gpd/claims/C-0086-seamless-scaffold-routing.md), task [`T-151`](gpd/tasks/T-151.md), raises [`CH-0101`](gpd/challenges/CH-0101-the-nominal-tile-width-is-not-a-buildable-raster-width.md). **YES — and a seam is a PARITY ON A TREE, not a fabrication convention.** Crossovers join only *adjacent* duplexes, so the row-adjacency graph is the path `P_D`, a **tree**, and a closed walk on a tree traverses every edge an **even** number of times: a **fully folded circular** scaffold therefore gives every row two segments, which is exactly Rothemund's seam. Brute-forced, `P_D` carries **2 Hamiltonian paths and ZERO Hamiltonian cycles** at every `D` from 3 to 12. **So a seam needs BOTH premises — circular AND fully folded — and the Gen-1 tile fails the second anyway**, taking **1 680** of M13's **7 249** nt. The seamless routing is the plain boustrophedon, and **Rothemund built it twice**: a 26-helix square that *"had no vertical reversals in raster direction, required a linear scaffold"*, and — his **first** origami experiment — an 8-helix raster on a **circular** M13 with two thirds unfolded, *"long, unfolded single-stranded sections of the scaffold do not adversely affect folding"*. Both read directly. **Three things the programme had not counted.** The **remainder** is a body: 5 569 unpaired nt is a **33.3 nm** coil carrying **1.66×** the sheet's own backbone charge in `C-0022`'s gap, and a purpose-built 1 680 nt scaffold removes it (a circular one needs a **67 nt** return loop). **Rothemund's own staggered seam is NOT the remedy** — solved exactly over the three-row coupling `C-0081`'s mechanism implies, the best stagger still costs **5 of 34** against a straight seam's 6–12, worth one station. And a seamless raster **quantises the tile's WIDTH at 32 bp**: the admissible row lengths are odd multiples of the 16 bp crossover spacing — 16, 48, 80, 112, 144 bp — and **§3's 40.0 nm (117.6 bp) is not among them**; the nearest is **112 bp = 38.08 nm**, 4.8 % narrower (`CH-0101`). F5 fired. `C-0081`'s 8 planes and 6–12 cost reproduce from its station planes alone. |

At those phases the **upward station lattice is bit-identical** (departure `0.0`), so the comparison is of hosts and loads. **The tile is FLATTER**: the best 34-root placement dishes **0.0621469105** of the stroke against `T-5b`'s 0.10, the free tile's 0.299035 and `C-0063`'s **0.0706145537** — **12.0 % better**, with the winning phase moving 24 → 8. **What it costs is that the arm must be an integer number of base pairs.** The plan budget is a **minimum of two bounds** and `C-0069` reported only the inboard one: the outboard `edgeX/2 − pitch` owns it below `edgeX = 2(2p − d) = 38.14 nm`, and 38.08 falls **0.176 bp** short of that. `C-0039`'s 8.16439083 nm root overhangs by **0.00439083 nm** (0.0129 of a rise), takes the phase-24 capacity 45 → 38, halves the family 198 288 → 93 312 and dishes **0.1427** at phase 8 — outside `T-5b`; `C-0085`'s **24-rise 8.16 nm** arm is *exactly* tangent (`32 + 24 = 56 = 112/2`) and restores every number bit for bit. **`C-0085`'s widening is annihilated** on a three-site row (8.84 → 8.16 nm, every clearance 4–8 rises giving 8.16), which is [`CH-0105`](gpd/challenges/CH-0105-the-plan-budget-is-two-bounds-and-only-one-carries-the-tile-width.md). `C-0053`'s in-plane packer is the largest mover, **43 → 29 arms**. **`CH-0101` is discharged and its sign guess corrected** — it calls the effect *"unfavourable but small"*. F1–F6 all silent; gate reproduces `C-0063`'s 0.0706145537 at **2.88e−10** and its 198 288/163 296 exactly. |

| T-161 | **Can a crossover be drawn at the LAST base pair of a duplex?** | A literature answer, or a design-language one, on whether a scaffold or staple crossover may sit at a row end — with the placement verdict it decides stated beside it | A8.2 | **DONE** (iteration 19) — claim [`C-0095`](gpd/claims/C-0095-row-end-crossover.md), task [`T-161`](gpd/tasks/T-161.md), result `gpd/results/T-161-row-end-crossover.json`, sources and query log `gpd/data/T-161-sources/`, raises [`CH-0111`](gpd/challenges/CH-0111-the-row-end-crossover-is-admitted-with-an-interior-crossover-s-stiffness.md), **closes `C-0090`'s open item 2**. **YES, AND IT IS NOT A PERMISSION — IT IS THE DEFINITION OF A RASTER TURN.** Rothemund's odd-half-turn rule binds *"the distance between successive scaffold crossovers"*, and in a boustrophedon those are the **two ends of one row**: the 112 bp `C-0086` quantised **is** the crossover-to-crossover distance, so a crossover at the last base pair is what makes the row 112 bp long. **The cheap bound was the whole answer and it cost a `grep`** — the primary source was already in the tree, fetched by `T-151`. **Three headings, decided separately.** *The geometry does not forbid it*: a duplex end carries **2** strand termini and the 33.74°/bp quantum lets one base pair reach **one** neighbour, so it offers **1** crossover — and a boustrophedon demands **exactly 1** at every row end it uses, leaving **two** free (the scaffold's own termini, one per edge on an odd row count). A crossover is an **azimuthal** condition and *"the last base pair"* an **axial** coordinate; they are independent, and the terminus is *less* constrained than an interior site. *The software does not forbid it*: caDNAno *"permits the user to force crossovers between any two staple bases or between any two scaffold bases"*, cadnano 2.1 **automates** the raster turn, and scadnano's API states *"an xover is necessarily at an enpoint of a strand"*. *And it is published*: **F5 FIRED, FAVOURABLY** — Rothemund's 24-helix rectangle is **288 bp = 18 column pitches EXACTLY** (Suppl. Fig. S19, *"27 turns wide at 10.666 bases / turn -> 288 nt"*), so **both** vertical edges lie on the crossover lattice, and it folded **90 % well-formed**. His Suppl. Note S2 contemplates the case verbatim — *"even where a seam or edge lines up with the underlying crossover lattice"* — and reports *"the last base pair does form and assumes a planar configuration"*. **The unexpected result is a theorem**: `C-0086`'s odd-half-turn rule and the row-end column **parity** condition are the SAME congruence, asserted identical over **1–400 bp** — a boustrophedon needs complementary parities at its two edges, and that is exactly an **odd** pitch count. So the eight columns at phase 8/24 carry `C-0015`'s **56** as **14 scaffold raster turns + 42 staple crossovers**, the 14 being `C-0086`'s own LINEAR count recovered from the lattice; and the model reproduces a structure it was not told about (an **even** pitch count puts one parity on both edges, which is what a seamed **double** raster demands). **VERDICT: the programme carries `C-0090`'s ADMITTED reading, 0.0621469105** — against **0.168371808** at the same phase (**2.70925468×**) and 0.156510532 at the best refused phase (2.51839602×) — so the 38.08 nm tile is **inside** `T-5b`'s 0.10 and **12.0 % flatter** than §3's nominal 40.0 nm at 0.0706145537. `CrossoverLayout.EDGE_MARGIN` is a numerical guard and must not be read as a physical assertion. **NO SIXTH NDI QUESTION** — the literature is not silent, and what is left is mechanics: Rothemund says the edge crossover's strain is unrelieved and *"how the strain is actually relieved is unknown"*, so the two *conventions* are in fact a stiffness **BRACKET** on 14 of the 56 crossovers, and `T-5b`'s 0.10 sits **0.356** of the way across it (`CH-0111`, `T-164`). F1–F4 and F6 silent; the upward station lattice is identical under both conventions (52 / 53). |

| T-135 | **Which output element does the Gen-1 programme recommend, and on what premises?** | A stated recommendation with its premises, its open items and its failure routes counted — or the statement that the programme cannot yet recommend one, with what would decide it | A8.2 | **DONE** (iteration 14) — claim [`C-0071`](gpd/claims/C-0071-output-element-recommendation.md), raises [`CH-0083`](gpd/challenges/CH-0083-the-pull-in-verdict-is-quoted-for-a-load-line-the-recommendation-does-not-use.md). **YES, and it recommends `C-0069`'s `Q5` — `C-0055`/`C-0063`'s hinge-rooted arm, 8.16439 nm on one antiparallel crossover, 34 instances at one level, at §3's ACCEPTABLE clause.** The decision needed **no new calculation**: 11 catalogued elements → **3 place** → **2 survive every clause**, and the three tie-break axes — undemonstrated motifs, `C-0017`'s six 2 mM floors cleared, compression members, each an **integer count a standing claim already carries** — are **unanimous** (1 v 2, 6 v 4, 0 v 1). The declared falsifier, *that the axes disagree*, **did not fire**. **The price is the claim.** **20 premises, 3 of them UNDEMONSTRATED** — the free lever on one crossover (`C-0055`, not found in 62 queries, upstream of the element itself), the normal-standing duplex (`C-0028`, upstream of the *decision* only), and that `C-0009`'s **in-plane-fitted** `k_θ` transfers to the **out-of-plane** azimuth, which no measurement covers. **No margin at all on 3 of 14 graded quantities** — plan length **1.00314×**, tip joint **1.01844×**, root joint **1.02964×** — **and the finding is that those three are ONE arithmetic**, `pitch − d − L = 0.0256 nm`, asserted equal to `1e−12`; a fourth `NONE`, `C-0066`'s free-standing tie, is the **same** number and is **discharged** by the tip registration (2.019×), **so `T-134` has one target and not two**. **Four specification questions still bind** (`T-63`, `P-13`, `T-115`, `T-112`) **and two are DISCHARGED BY THIS ELEMENT** — `T-95` and `T-102`, both raised by the flexure-and-tie branch `CH-0081` removed from the output role, **and nobody noticed at the time**. **Nine failure routes: 5 remove the element, 2 of those 5 are ALREADY INSIDE A PUBLISHED BRACKET** (the square lattice's 2.73 nm → 18 of 34; `α = 1.2` → 30 of 34) **and 1 — the motif — can be settled only at a bench.** **No §6 verdict moves**; task 4 is the one the recommendation does not discharge for itself, because its own pull-in fold has never been computed (`CH-0083`). |
| T-129 | Is `C-0063`'s flat placement flat over a RANGE? | `T-123`'s multi-state minimax re-run on `C-0055`/`C-0063`'s 34 upward roots, over the states each device traverses, against `T-5b`'s 10 % — or the statement that the placement is a single-state result | A8.2 | **DONE** (iteration 13) — claim [`C-0068`](gpd/claims/C-0068-range-robust-placement.md), raises [`CH-0080`](gpd/challenges/CH-0080-the-equal-spring-advantage-belongs-to-the-ten-nanometre-layer.md) against `C-0063`'s Deliverable 2. **YES for the device the programme placed, and only for a 10 nm layer.** With **34 equal springs** and `C-0017`'s unchanged total, `C-0063`'s placement dishes **0.0789** over the whole range `C-0018`'s placed 2 mM device traverses (gaps 10 → 7 nm at its own 0.192 V, both ends solved by `C-0022`), **0.0853** at `C-0032`'s 0.5 mM and **0.0896** at 10 mM — **all three inside `T-5b`'s 0.10**, against **0.0706** at the single state. **So the one-state verdict travels, and what the range costs is the margin**: 1.42× becomes **1.12×**, spent at the *compressed* end. **The exception is the 5 nm device** — the one whose range owns `C-0022`'s 2 nm state: equal springs dish **0.2000**, 2.0× outside, and are **worse than no coupling at all at both of its states** (0.1104 against a free 0.0638 at 5 nm; 0.2000 against 0.1648 at 2 nm), where the same equal springs on the 3 × 15 grid are inside it at 0.0796. **A distribution recovers all four** (0.0291 / 0.0365 / **0.0565** / 0.0382) at peak ratios of only **1.72–2.32**, far inside what `C-0060` prices — so this is a scope correction, not an infeasibility, and it is `CH-0080`. **The placement is vindicated twice, and the phase turns out to be what the LAYER selects**: re-enumerating the centro-symmetric family **exhaustively under a range objective** (361 584 placements, each priced under two objectives) finds **0 of 198 288** at phase 24 better than `C-0063`'s own, which *is* the range argmin — while under the **5 nm** device's range **nothing at phase 24 clears** (best 0.1169) and a **phase-8** placement does with equal springs (**0.0895**), reading 0.2416 at the 10 nm design state. Two interpolated intermediate gaps move the design reading by **exactly zero**, and the tightest range's 41/81/161 sampling spread is 0.08 %. **The cheap bound did not fire** (per-state floor 0.0056, 18× below) and `C-0064`'s sign instrument explains it: a 10 nm device's own two ends run **+0.9969 to +0.9998**, while the 5 nm device's two states run **−0.9427** — a device whose range is anti-parallel to itself. `C-0064`'s subset dichotomy transfers in **direction, not exactness**: 12 of the 14 mixed subsets fail (against 14 of 14 on the grid). ~~TODO — **high, and it is the cheapest open item on the branch.**~~ `C-0063` makes the Gen-1 tile flat at **0.0706** of the stroke with **equal springs** on a placement the upward lattice actually supplies, which resolves `CH-0074` — but it reports that at **one** state, `C-0022`'s 2 mM / 10 nm / 0.192 V design point, which is the same state `C-0058` was tuned at. `C-0064` (`T-123`) is the reason that is not enough: a flatness verdict at one state does not travel, the uniform coupling on the 3 × 15 grid moves from 0.2182 to 0.0710 across `C-0022`'s five, and the 2 nm state's edge effect has the **opposite sign** to every 10 nm state's. Everything needed exists: `coupling.MultiStateSurrogate` prices every state of one distribution on `n + S` load cases and `minimaxStiffnessDistribution` optimises the worst of them, both model-agnostic; `anchoring.UpwardRootPlacement` supplies the stations. Three questions in one run — is `C-0063`'s **winning placement** flat over the 2 mM device's traversed range; if not, does a **different phase** of the same lattice do better (the phase sweep is over a *placement*, and `C-0063` shows all ten eight-column phases reach 0.077–0.092 at one state); and does the **34-root** array need a distribution after all, given `C-0058`'s rim rule reverses sign there and `C-0063`'s own 34-parameter optimum wants a peak ratio of only 1.30. The falsifier is that the best placement misses `T-5b`'s 0.10 over the range, in which case the flat Gen-1 tile is a single-state result and `CH-0074` reopens on a new axis. |

The threshold: an imperforate superstructure caps the stroke at `ℓ − 2.69 nm`, i.e. **5.31 nm at the recommended `ℓ = 8 nm`**, which covers §3's acceptable clause and nothing more. **Carried to the open questions for Kazik as item 6.** |
| T-96 | Does the surviving mounting survive `T-31`'s array packing? | 45 flexures of ~32 nm span placed in plan, with their 45 apertures, on a body the size of the tile | A8.2 | **DONE** (iteration 6) — claim `C-0041`, challenge `CH-0055`. **It does not, at EITHER stroke, and it does not fail on area.** The cheap bound is 2.59× the footprint with the standoff feet — exactly the size that invites *"stack it in three levels"*, and `C-0017`'s envelope has room for three (`ℓ = 5.78 / 7.82 / 9.86 nm`). **Stacking buys nothing**: a standoff runs from the superstructure to its own beam plane and a tie from that plane to the tile, so any two vertical members share a height range whatever levels their beams sit at — the clash is **level-independent**. Two lattice facts meet nowhere: the grid's **across-helix pitch IS one duplex**, so beams in adjacent rows are tangent at zero tilt and mutually bury each other's ties at any other; and its **along-helix pitch, 13.33 nm, is under the span plus a duplex, 34.51 nm**, so a row's beams bury each other's standoff **feet**. **0 of 720 orientations, at any level count, on any body** — for 3 × 15 and for 2 × 15. The tile carries **exactly fifteen**: 1 × 15, span **21.44 nm = 63 bp**, feasible at **exactly one** of 720 orientations and that one supplied by the sheet's own helix direction. **The two clauses differ in KIND** — at 3 nm the binding variable is the **path count** (45 → 15, free: 6.67 pN/path against 10 pN, 2.16× buckling margin, tangent 25.49 pN/nm); at 10 nm the count is bounded **below at 29** by the same allowable and **above at 15** by the packing, so the window is **empty** and the binding variable is the **footprint**: **≥ 2330 nm², 1.44×, 1.20× in edge**. And the tie apertures are not 45 holes but `m` collinear slots that **sever** the superstructure into **18 components** at every one of the 32 phases — cured by **8 bp of stagger**. |
| T-101 | Is a 15-attachment scheme flat under the SOLVED load? | The dishing of a 1 × 15 grid under `C-0022`'s solved profile, against `T-5b`'s 10 % convention | A8.2 | **DONE** (iteration 7) — claim `C-0047`, raises `CH-0060`. **NO, and it is not `CH-0034`'s saturation — it is a LOSS.** 1 × 15 dishes **0.695 of the stroke**, **7.0×** `T-5b`'s convention, against 0.218 at 3 × 15 and 0.223 at 8 × 8; the criterion does not saturate below 45, and 15 sits **4.7× above** `CH-0034`'s 0.149 floor. **Below three columns a coupling is a NET DISHING SOURCE** — the free, uncoupled tile dishes 0.308, so 1 × 15 is **2.26× worse than no coupling at all**, break-even at exactly the three columns `C-0041` shows cannot be built. The **cheap bound** saw it in four operations: the along-helix Winkler bending length is **12.83 nm** against a 40 nm column pitch (3.12) and a 2.69 nm row pitch against 5.71 nm across (0.47). Orientation is worth 1.08× in dishing and **16× on the crossover path**, and the packing forces the good angle. The 8 bp stagger costs +2.19 % of the dishing and **breaks `C-0015`'s exact zero at first order** (0.389 pN, `CH-0060`); swept as a *design variable* it buys **45 %** back at ±13.60 nm — the bending length to 6 % — and still does not reach the tolerance. `C-0022`'s 32.1 % split is **unaffected**; what moves is the sag term, 32 % of the dishing at 3 × 15 → **79 %** at 1 × 15. |
| T-113 | Can a NON-UNIFORM coupling stiffness buy back the edge dishing? | The dishing of a 1 × 15 and a 3 × 15 grid with the rim springs stiffened, against `T-5b`'s 10 % convention | A8.2 | **DONE** (iteration 9) — claim [`C-0058`](gpd/claims/C-0058-non-uniform-coupling.md), raises [`CH-0071`](gpd/challenges/CH-0071-the-saturation-floor-is-a-property-of-the-equal-spring-family.md) against `CH-0034`. **YES at three columns, NO at one, and it is the first time anything in this programme has made the Gen-1 tile flat.** At 45 paths, redistributing `C-0017`'s **same** 33.3333 pN/nm — no extra stiffness, no extra attachments — takes the dishing from **0.2182** of the stroke to **0.0753** under a one-parameter rule (*the 34 stations within 6.7 nm of an edge carry 5× the other 11*) and **0.0544** under a 45-parameter optimisation, both inside `T-5b`'s 0.10, where `CH-0034`'s **count** axis saturates at 0.149. So **0.149 is the saturation of the EQUAL-SPRING family, not a rim floor** (`CH-0071`). **The cost is a force and it is affordable at 45 paths**: 2.762 pN per path at the 3 nm stroke against the 10 pN unzip allowable (3.62× clear), 0.784 pN in the worst crossover, 1.13 pN of duplex shear, and +24 % on `C-0014`'s thermal force — which is **linear** in a path's share, not its square root. **The per-path ceiling costs nothing at 45 paths** (capped and uncapped optima coincide) and binds at 15. **At `C-0041`'s 1 × 15 the axis fails**: 13.0 % admissible improvement, still 6.0× the tolerance and **1.96× worse than no coupling at all** — fifteen springs on one line can reweight a placement, not move it. **But flatness bought this way is owed at ONE state**: the same design is flat at three of `C-0022`'s five solved states and dishes 0.187 at the 2 nm gap where the *uniform* coupling dishes 0.071, and a minimax over all five reaches only 0.1587. Two smaller results: the break-even moves from three columns to **two**, and the lattice/plate excess **changes sign with the distribution** (−8 % uniform, +24 % at the flat design). Opens `T-122` (is a 5:1 per-path stiffness ratio buildable?) and `T-123` (is any distribution flat at every state?). ~~TODO — **medium-high, and it is the last unexplored axis `C-0047` leaves.**~~ Every attachment in the corpus is an *equal* spring: `C-0017`'s mandate fixes the **total** 33.3333 pN/nm and nothing upstream requires it to be shared equally. `C-0047` shows the dishing at fifteen paths is 79 % the coupling's own sag and that `C-0022`'s rim collar is where the load actually is, so stiffening the two rim rows (or the rim *stations* of a multi-column grid) at constant total is free of every standing claim and has never been run. The cheap bound is that the load is 1.3–1.9× the interior value inside an 8.9 nm collar, so the matched stiffness distribution is knowable before the solve. **It cannot rescue 1 × 15** — a single column has no rim stations along `x` — **but it is the only remaining way to attack `CH-0034`'s 0.149 floor**, which no attachment *count* touches. |

But `C-0041` checked severance, plan-view fit and the 720-orientation sweep **at 8 bp only**, and a 6.75× larger stagger moves every tie aperture, every standoff foot and every beam centre. Three things need re-running and none is expensive: the union-find (does a large stagger still leave one component, and does it leave *more* material intact?), the plan-view blocking (beams at alternating `x` are no longer collinear, which may **relieve** `C-0041`'s Fact B), and `C-0047`'s own 2.58 pN crossover force against the 10 pN unzip allowable at every one of `C-0022`'s 21 states. **If the large stagger relieves Fact B it is not only a flatness gain — it is a second column by another name**, and `C-0047`'s break-even says two columns is still worse than none while three is better. |

| T-106 | The truss cap as a solved body, not a series spring | The cap's own geometry and rotational compliance, and `C-0037`'s frame couple recomputed on it | A8.2 | **DONE** (iteration 7) — claim `C-0048`, raises `CH-0061`. **The cap cannot be the flexure and cannot be a spring.** `C-0042`'s own two functions settle the geometry before any solve: the steric floor puts two legs `≥ 2R` apart and a leg is seated only within `R` of a seat's axis, so a duplex laid ACROSS the row seats **neither** (line contact **0.000 nm** at 6–16 bp, against 2.000 along it) — and the counting theorem says it again, one link per leg. **The cap is a separate crossbar duplex, 13 bp = 4.38 nm, hosting THREE 90° junctions, six links, its axis one radius above the leg heads.** Its *bending* is the small term (`12EI/w`, 8.93× the couple, so the frame couple falls only 74.18 → **71.31** pN·nm/rad at 7 bp — but 42.5 % at 16 bp, because it goes as `1/w` where the couple goes as `w²`); its torsion 0.1 %; its **height** raises `C12` and shortens the legs 1 nm. **What moves the answer is that `C-0037` applies `C-0029`'s counting theorem to the head's AXIAL path and takes its ROTATION as infinite** — 78.24 / 13.53 on a conserved 91.76, a **ceiling** on the head restraint, not a series correction. So the cap junction's azimuth is a **third** instance of one rank-one budget and, unlike the leg row's, it has **no free corner**: laid along the flexure axis the free plane caps at 6.20 pN and **no** separation to 16 bp crosses; laid across it, **7 bp** crosses again. **`C-0042`'s seven base pairs stands and its reason does not — the crossing is bought at the cap, not at the row.** All nine predicates PASS over `h = 5–10 nm` on both rigidities, at **1.95 / 1.46** against `C-0037`'s 2.79 / 2.10: the cap costs **30 %** of the margin, 38 % of the draw-in supply and 35 % of the tangent headroom, and `k_s`/32 now fails on **both** rigidities. Opens `T-109` (three junctions on one crossbar). |
| T-117 | Do THREE 90° junctions close on one crossbar duplex? | `C-0042`'s closure search at the other end of the same legs: two leg heads from below and the flexure's own end from the side, on a **lone** 13 bp seat with no lattice neighbours | A8.2 | **DONE** (iteration 8) — claim `C-0052`, raises `CH-0067`. **THEY CLOSE**, at `C-0048`'s own 13 bp and at every length to 19 bp and every row from 6 to 12: six links inside the measured `[0.60, 0.70]` nm step, **zero unpaired nucleotides**, six distinct targets, seat contacts 1.83–1.96 nm against `C-0042`'s 1.60 floor, and the wanted chord alignment at **0.00°** on the converged grid — and it still closes at **14 of 15** integer leg lengths with the legs' azimuths *carried up from `C-0042`'s base* rather than searched. Two cheap bounds were not formalities: the axial **rim** leaves exactly **0.02 nm** per end at every row width (`2R` is 5.88 rises and `ceil` buys 6 — and *"13 bp = 4.38 nm"* is a demand and a length, 13 bp being 4.42), and the **flexure's end passes a leg's surface at 0.249 nm**, tighter than the 0.54–0.69 nm gaps packed origami keeps, with the row width the only lever (0.184 at 6 bp, 0.714 at 12). **The finding is that a LEG IS ONE BODY WITH TWO JUNCTIONS**: its base and cap chords differ by `m × 33.74°`, so `C-0048`'s recommended 7.00 nm leg (21 steps) is **78.5°** from the azimuth pair its design is written on — and on the wrong side at 10.67, 10.5 **and** 10.4 bp/turn, with 13 steps the only length surviving all three. Only the *difference* is quantised (rotating the leg trades the two misalignments one for one), so **every** quantised length still passes all nine predicates at **1.81–2.43 / 1.36–1.83** — and at 21 steps the forced pair is **better** than the recommended one (2.20/1.66 against 1.95/1.46), because `C-0048` chose its cap azimuth on the **free** plane while the **loaded** one governs. The crossbar is a duplex: **31 `k_BT`** sequence-averaged and **17** at the worst sequence at 2 mM MgCl₂ on Huguet et al.'s measured magnesium nearest-neighbour set, and overhang is mechanically free because `12EI/w` and `4C/w` carry the **row**. |
| T-107 | Is `C-0023`'s 40 pN/nm compliance ceiling required at the DESIRED stroke, or only at the working point? | The ceiling re-derived from `C-0017`/`C-0018` with the stroke it is read at named | A8.2 | **DONE** (iteration 7) — claim `C-0049`, challenge `CH-0062`. **It is owed at the PLACEMENT stroke, 3 nm, and nowhere else** — 40 is exactly `1.2 x (100 pN / 3 nm)`, a **declared linearity tolerance on the placement discharge** that carries its stroke inside it, and the *same* construction at §3's desired clause is **12 pN/nm, not 40**. **Neither `C-0017` nor `C-0018` contains an upper bound on a coupling tangent**: placement is an EQUALITY on the secant, stability a FLOOR on the tangent, and `C-0032` measures a stiffer tangent *raising* `C-0018`'s pull-in margin. What binds beyond the working point is `C-0006`'s **per-path unzip allowable**, a bound on a FORCE, so as a stiffness it is `n·allowable/s` and **tightens as 1/s**: 150 → 45 pN/nm at 45 paths, 50 → **15** at `C-0041`'s buildable 15. `C-0039`'s `E5a16` secant at 10 nm (69.94) is past both, so the miss moves from **6.6x on a declared tolerance to 1.55x on a cited allowable** and **no verdict moves**. **And the same answer settles `CH-0047`/`T-76b`**: a device placed at 3 nm traverses `[0, 3]` and never occupies the 4.556 nm stroke `CH-0042`'s minimum is taken at — read at the placement stroke `C-0030`'s flexure is 25.227 pN/nm and clears **4 of `C-0017`'s 6** 2 mM floors, where 22.875 clears **none**. |
| T-108 | Is §3's DESIRED 10 nm stroke reachable by any coupling this programme has? | A synthesis over every element in the catalogue, or the plain statement that 10 nm is out of reach and 3 nm is not | A8.2 | **DONE** (iteration 7) — claim `C-0050`. **NO, and the bound that settles it contains no coupling at all.** The stroke is `s = L₀ − h`, so **`s < L₀` identically**, and §3 names no layer taller than 10 nm: a 10 nm stroke on a 10 nm layer IS `h = 0`. The best phases are `C-0015`'s **ten centro-symmetric** ones, for the third time. **And the two missing arms are the least of it**: an arm is *cut out of* the host, so 43 arms take **65.4 %** of its duplex length, spend **43** crossovers and bury **13** more — **56 of 56, exactly 100 %** — leaving **zero** surviving crossovers and **no bonded component at all**. Self-consistent count **43**; the count that leaves the host whole is **25** (15 duplexes bonded at 25, 14 at 30, 8 at 35, 3 at 40, none at 42), so the threshold is **45 → 25, 1.80×**. 45 never place on **any** of five swept axes (the double-nick reading of the root gives 30). One thing improves: the lattice carries **three** columns, exactly `C-0047`'s break-even, so this array is **not** a net dishing source where `C-0041`'s 1 × 15 is 2.26× worse than nothing. |

| T-70 | What holds `E5g16`'s guided arm, and does `c = 12` survive its own anchorage? | The second anchorage's rotational compliance, and the arm cap `(c n EI/k)^(1/3)` recomputed on the realised `c` | A8.2 | **DONE** (iteration 5) — claim `C-0034`, raises `CH-0044`. **`c = 12` does NOT survive — the realised factor is 6.28–7.36 — and the cap does not need it to.** The end condition is a continuum, **`c(ρ) = 12(1+ρ)/(4+ρ)`**, exactly 3 at a free far end and 12 at a guided one and exactly **6 at `ρ = 2`**; and because **`ρ_far = k_far r/EI` carries the ARM**, the cap is a **fixed point**, `r = (c(k_far r/EI) n EI/k)^(1/3)`, so a longer arm buys its own guidance. **Every TWO-link anchorage clears §3's desired 10 nm stroke** — 13.43 nm at the arm's own duplex-end couple, 15.18 at a singly nicked continuation, 15.44 at a two-crossover clamp, and **10.97 nm even about the CHORD**, the axis the counting theorem leaves free (and which here is unloaded, because the arm carries no compression). **A ONE-link anchorage is exactly the cantilever, 9.77 nm, and fails** — and that is Rothemund's own observed failure. Design: **`E5a16`, an 11.03–12.50 nm = 32–37 bp arm on 16 crossovers**, tangent 33.56/36.78 at 3/10 nm, 1.83 pN per crossover, anchorage couple 16.7 pN per link = **7.3 bp** on `CH-0029`'s ladder. **The dominant compliance has changed sides: the ARM carries 58.5 %, not the hinge.** `CH-0044`: `c = 12` and `C-0023`'s series composition cannot both be right — the composition is exact **iff the far end carries no moment**, and `E5g16` read on its own `c = 12` is **1.64× over-placed**. |

Four of the eighteen are excluded by a **closed-form reach bound** — they close at *no* torsion: `C-0042`'s two 7 bp legs would need an `O3′–P` bond of **0.2517 / 0.2460 nm** against a covalent **0.16022 ± 0.00191**. The rest fail on **population**: `C-0029`'s binding link needs **ε = −22.9°** (0.015 % of 13 084 measured linkages) and its second **β = 27.4°** (**0 of 15 457** residues). **But a torsion-feasible placement EXISTS in `C-0029`'s own space and nobody was looking**: of **69 120** placements, 3 546 close on distance, **1 855** survive the reach bound and **18 of the 100 solved** close at torsion level — at gaps near **0.690 nm**, the *interior* of the window a residual-minimising search never visits. The scaffold excursion is **12.7× rarer** (280 covalent, 1 of 100 closing). Every constant is measured here from **876** X-ray DNA-only PDB entries and reproduces Parkinson's restraint library to ≤ 1.6° and Svozil's BI to ≤ 7.1°; the `[0.60, 0.70]` nm window this programme cites has **no primary source** and measures **0.607 / 0.664**. |
| T-72 | Re-price `T-66`'s triangulated standoff as a **stability** remedy, not a rigidity one | Whether a second standoff restores the free axis at an affordable cost in sway | A8.2 | **DONE** (iteration 6) — claim `C-0037`, raises `CH-0050`, **covers `T-66`**. **YES, and the trade does not exist in the form it was posed.** The draw-in lives on `(u_x, phi_y)` and the buckling that closed the branch lives on `(u_y, phi_x)`; a frame couple is a **rank-one tensor on the leg offsets**, so for a two-leg row at azimuth `theta`, `Sigma x^2 = (w^2/2)cos^2 theta` and `Sigma y^2 = (w^2/2)sin^2 theta` — **conserved, and at 90 degrees the loaded plane inherits EXACTLY ZERO**. Two legs laid **ACROSS** the flexure axis take the adopted critical load **1.46 -> 9.77 pN (6.71x)**, move the governing plane from free to loaded — restoring `C-0029`'s restrained-axis reading, which is exactly what `C-0029` said a truss would have to do — and cost **23 % of the draw-in supply** (2.90x the demand, against 3.75x) and **3.5 % of the tangent** (26.09 against a 40 pN/nm ceiling). **The window is `l = 5-10 nm`, all nine predicates, on BOTH rigidities**, where the single standoff fails `P6` at every length (0.40-0.57) and buckles at a 3.05-7.02 nm stroke. **Laid ALONG the axis the same two legs still fail `P6`** and lose 63 % of the supply — worse on every axis at once. **The cost is the leg COUNT, not the leg SPACING**: `L2a6`/`L2a8`/`L2a12` are bit-identical in the loaded plane. Design: **`L2a8` — two 8 nm = 24 bp legs at 8 bp = 2.72 nm across the flexure axis, span 33.43 nm = 98 bp, tangent 26.09 pN/nm, 180 standoffs = 45 % of a tile footprint**. |

Declared falsifier **`F3` did not fire**, and it was declared against exactly this: the opposite outcome would have made the coupling the thing to design and the tile the thing to accept, and the design order is the other way round. **`C-0109`'s standing comparison is RE-FRAMED rather than contradicted**: *"every coupled cell is worse than the uncoupled tile"* is true and is **not a design verdict**, because §3 requires 100 pN to reach a load, so `C-0017`'s mandate is an **equality on the SUM** and the total is fixed and non-zero **by specification** — the uncoupled tile is a **reference, never a candidate**, asserted as a test (`equalShareOfMandate` refuses a zero total *and* a zero path count). **More paths are monotonically worse on `10 × 6`** (0.0278431488 → 0.0541089284 → 0.0461988976 → 0.0408747025 over 1 → 2 → 3 → 5 columns), which is *a dropout is an increase in the attachment pitch* read from the other side: a tile stiff enough not to need support pays only for the paths it has. Convergence **0.0** over 2 000 → 4 000 realisations on a **common stream**; reproduces `C-0120`'s uncoupled references at 5.9e−10 and 1.8e−10. **The largest thing left open is a census**: the attachment grid here is the abstract one, and every plan ceiling in this corpus is single-layer **square**-lattice, so a path count is a **request** rather than a demonstration that the honeycomb supplies the stations (`C-0119`). `C-0089`'s percentile descent and `C-0093`'s shared body are **not** run and could only improve cells that are already flat. |
| T-198 | **Can four honeycomb layers be RASTERED from one circular M13 at a buildable width?** | The admissible seamless row lengths of a **honeycomb** multilayer raster and a routing that closes on 60 duplexes — or the statement that a seam is unavoidable and what it costs | A8.2 | **DONE** (iteration 25) — claim [`C-0119`](gpd/claims/C-0119-honeycomb-raster-width.md), task [`T-198`](gpd/tasks/T-198-honeycomb-raster-width.md), result `gpd/results/T-198-honeycomb-raster-width.json`, tool [`tools/T-198-honeycomb-raster.py`](tools/T-198-honeycomb-raster.py) (29 self-tests). **YES — AND THE CROSS-SECTION IS NOT A PROPOSAL: it is design (i) of the caDNAno paper**, 15 × 4 in Douglas et al.'s own nomenclature (15 x-raster rows × 4 helices = **60** duplexes), folded from **p8064** and one of only **three of seven** to produce sharp leading monomer bands. **And that paper recommends a different one** — *"six-helices-per-x-raster row yields the greatest fraction of defect-free objects"*, i.e. **10 × 6** — so **the tile's aspect ratio is a design variable with published yield evidence and this programme has never treated it as one** (`T-199`). **`C-0086`'s odd-half-turn rule genuinely does not transfer** (a honeycomb half turn is 5.25 bp and no odd multiple is ever an integer) **but that is a DOMAIN ERROR, not a prohibition**: the honeycomb quantises its half turn to **5 bp**, so the scaffold lattice is `7k ± 5` — integral, `{2, 5, 9, 12, 16, 19, 23, …}` on a 112 bp row. **A seam is still forced, for a reason in the SOURCE rather than the geometry**: a honeycomb helix has three neighbours, so the adjacency graph has cycles and the tree-parity argument should die — but Figure 2b states *"the path of the scaffold stays within a 2D surface"*, so the graph the scaffold may use is a **path** and the parity survives unchanged. Brute-forced at every order 3–7 (a covering closed walk exists, every edge is traversed an **even** number of times, **no** Hamiltonian cycle, exactly **two** Hamiltonian paths) and a theorem beyond — the routine **refuses** past order 9, a guard added because the 60-vertex call was made and 59! does not announce itself. The published blocks show it: the paper's own TEM criterion excludes defects *"more than 3 nm away from the unpaired scaffold loops at the front and rear interfaces"*. **The scaffold budget re-checks**: `C-0109`'s 6 720 of 7 249 nt / 92.7 % reproduces, but this cross-section is actually folded from **p8064 = 8 064 nt**, so the remainder is **1 344 nt, not 529** — `T-195`'s body is 2.5× larger than it assumed. **The whole literature answer was four greps over a source `T-151` fetched two iterations ago, and ZERO fetches** — the fourth time *check `gpd/data/` first* has paid. |
| T-199 | **Is 10 × 6 a better tile than 15 × 4? The cross-section its own source recommends** | `C-0109`'s rigidities, `C-0116`'s threshold and `C-0022`'s collar re-read on a **10 × 6** honeycomb block — the same 60 duplexes and the same scaffold — against `T-5b`'s 0.10 | A8.2 | **DONE** (iteration 25) — claim [`C-0120`](gpd/claims/C-0120-cross-section-comparison.md), task [`T-199`](gpd/tasks/T-199-cross-section-comparison.md), result `gpd/results/T-199-cross-section-comparison.json`, study `tile.CrossSectionComparisonStudyKt`, 6 tests written first. **THE CROSS-SECTION ITS SOURCE RECOMMENDS ON YIELD IS ALSO THE ONE THAT REMOVES THIS PROGRAMME'S LAST UNMEASURED DEPENDENCY ON FLATNESS.** `10 × 6` dishes **0.00874363524** against `15 × 4`'s **0.0577199433** — **6.6× flatter at the same 60 helices** — and it has **NO composite-fraction threshold at all**: its dishing never reaches 0.10 anywhere in `f ∈ [0, 1]`, **including `f = 0` where the layers are fully independent**. So its flatness does not depend on the interlayer-coupling calibration, which `C-0116` names as the one unmeasured number the `15 × 4` verdict rests on (*"it would have to fall below 30 % of the least-coupled measured bundle"*). **The stronger cross-section is stronger by REMOVING a dependency, not by widening a margin.** `F1` and `F3` did not fire — the paper's yield recommendation and this programme's flatness criterion **agree**, which was not expected. **The cost is a SPECIFICATION one**: at the fixed 112 bp span, `15 × 4` is 38.08 × 38.04 nm (essentially §3's ~40 × 40) and `10 × 6` is 38.08 × 25.36 nm — **0.666666667** of the footprint, over which §3's 100 pN is specified and on which `C-0022`'s collar was **not** solved. **And Kirchhoff degrades**: thickness/span goes 0.252 → 0.386, so every `D_∥` is a weaker upper bound. The cheap bound predicted the direction with no plate at all — `Σy²` = `n(n²−1)d²/12`, so the excess scales as `(n²−1)` and six layers carry **2.3333×** four's, a pure integer function with no material constant. `6 × 10` and `3 × 20` are flatter still and are recommended by **nothing** — both folded, neither produced a sharp monomer band — which is the clearest demonstration that folding and flatness are independent criteria and both are needed. Reproduces `C-0116` at 3.2e−10 or better. Successors: re-solve `C-0022`'s collar at the new aspect ratio, and run `T-197` on whichever cross-section survives. |

| T-203 | **What attachment lattice does a honeycomb block's top face offer?** | The upward station census of an `m × n` honeycomb block, and whether it supplies the path counts `C-0118`'s flat coupled cells ask for | A8.2 | **DONE** (iteration 26) — claim [`C-0122`](gpd/claims/C-0122-honeycomb-station-lattice.md), task [`T-203`](gpd/tasks/T-203-honeycomb-station-lattice.md), result `gpd/results/T-203-honeycomb-station-lattice.json`, study `tile.HoneycombStationLatticeStudyKt`, model `tile/HoneycombStationLattice.kt`, 8 tests written first. **`C-0118`'S LARGEST CAVEAT IS DISCHARGED: the honeycomb supplies the stations, and the flatness survives being placed on the real lattice.** All **8** demands fit — `15 × 4` offers **90** upward stations and `10 × 6` offers **60**, against 10–75 asked — and snapping every station to the honeycomb's own **21 bp = 7.140 nm** ladder leaves **all four `10 × 6` cells flat at p90**, three of them slightly *better*. **Neither falsifier fired.** **The trap the census turns on is inside one sentence of the source**: 7 bp is *any* azimuth's next position and **21 bp** is *one* azimuth's period, and an attachment roots on **one** azimuth — a factor of three, asserted as a test. **And the census carries a finding nobody was looking for: a deeper block offers FEWER stations.** At a fixed 60 helices the count is set by the **face**, not the helix count, so the flatter, threshold-free `10 × 6` supplies **60** where `15 × 4` supplies **90** — **a thicker tile buys rigidity and spends attachment lattice**, the opposite of what a reader expects, and the one place `C-0120`'s recommendation costs something *structural* rather than specificational. Not binding (the best cell needs ten) but it is the axis on which a future coupling runs out of room. **Open, and raised rather than answered**: half the top-face helices carry only **oblique** free azimuths (7 of 15, 5 of 10) and this corpus has never priced an oblique root against a perpendicular one. **A defect found by the claim's own link check became a fourth retained checker** — see `P-25`. |
| T-204 | **Does `C-0022`'s collar transfer to the `10 × 6` aspect ratio?** | The collar's share re-derived at each cross-section's own perimeter-to-area ratio, and the flat cells re-graded at the scaled collar | A7.4, with A8.2 | **DONE** (iteration 26) — claim [`C-0123`](gpd/claims/C-0123-collar-aspect-ratio.md), task [`T-204`](gpd/tasks/T-204-collar-aspect-ratio.md), result `gpd/results/T-204-collar-aspect-ratio.json`, study `tile.CollarAspectRatioStudyKt`, 3 tests written first. **THE COLLAR TRANSFER CANNOT MOVE THE `10 × 6` VERDICT, AND THE BOUND NEEDED NO FIELD SOLVE.** The collar is **local** — depth and width set by screening — so its **share** scales as **perimeter over area**: `15 × 4` carries **1.05555×** what `C-0022`'s solved 40 × 40.35 nm tile does and `10 × 6` carries **1.31958×**, a **1.25013×** difference between them.
