| T-206 | **What does an OBLIQUE attachment root cost against a perpendicular one?** | The stiffness of a root on an oblique honeycomb azimuth against one on a perpendicular azimuth, or the statement that the difference is not representable in this programme's joint models | A8.2 | **DONE** (iteration 28) — claim [`C-0128`](gpd/claims/C-0128-oblique-attachment-root.md), challenge [`CH-0151`](gpd/challenges/CH-0151-an-oblique-helix-has-two-free-azimuths-not-one.md), task [`T-206`](gpd/tasks/T-206-oblique-root.md), result `gpd/results/T-206-oblique-root.json`, study `tile.ObliqueRootStudyKt`, model `tile/ObliqueRoot.kt`, 23 tests written first. **THE COST IS ONE ANISOTROPY AND THE CHEAP BOUND SETTLED THE WHOLE SHAPE OF THE ANSWER BEFORE A CONSTANT WAS READ.** `κ(ψ) = cos²ψ + sin²ψ·A` with `A = k_radial/k_tangential`, so at the honeycomb's own **60°** — *derived* as half the lattice's azimuth separation, not asserted — the cost is `0.25 + 0.75 A`: **three quarters of the load path is the tangential axis whatever the radial one is**, `κ ≥ 1` iff `A ≥ 1`, and it is **exactly 1** iff the root is isotropic. **Three root models, three answers, and the split is not a bracket**: a **flexible tie** is isotropic by `FlexureEndJoint`'s own invariant, so it costs **exactly nothing at every azimuth** (a symmetry, not a small number); a **crossover-hinged rigid body** costs **6.01719478×** (link against `C-0009`'s dihedral spring on the `d/2` lever); and read as this corpus reads a covalent tie everywhere else — a **constraint**, a binary — the perpendicular root has no stiffness and the **ratio is NOT REPRESENTABLE**. **The ABSOLUTE survives that boundary where the ratio cannot**: **10.7534964** against **11.2196457 pN/nm**, **1.04335×** apart, while the perpendicular reading moves **4096×** over `Gen1Tile`'s own four-decade sweep of the one constant nobody has measured — so the oblique root is quotable **without settling `T-9`**. **`C-0037`'s 45° guard was checked and does NOT apply**: it is on a *couple*'s chord misalignment, where the axes exchange; this is a *translation* azimuth and `k_z(ψ)` is smooth over the whole quadrant. **`C-0118`'s FLAT CELLS SURVIVE** — re-graded with the alternation the lattice imposes, **4 of 4 `10 × 6` cells stay flat at p90** under the measured dropout, all four equal-spring rows reproducing `C-0118` at departure **exactly 0.0** (its own seed, deliberately), and the **paired** median cost of the alternation is **0.04–0.56 %** against the **2.80 %** an unpaired p90-ratio reports — *a ratio of two order statistics is not the order statistic of the ratio*. Where the root does cost, the remedy is a **spacer contour length** (1.37456931× stiffer, quantised at a nucleotide), and a design using one staple length everywhere pays a **10.268949 %** shortfall on `C-0017`'s mandated **sum** — a specification failure, not a flatness one. **And two roots on one rigid head add as STIFFNESS TENSORS**: **44.9750426 pN/nm** against the scalar sum's **21.5069928**, **2.0911823×**, recovering a perpendicular root to **1.43870642×** for two stations. `CH-0151` is raised against `C-0122`'s **count** — an oblique helix has **two** free azimuths, so the census is **132**/`15 × 4` and **90**/`10 × 6`, 1.4667× and 1.5× — **favourable**, and every `C-0122` verdict survives. `CH-0152` reserved and **NOT used**: no second contradiction was found. |

| T-216 | **What is the crossover PHASE LATTICE of a mixed-domain row?** | The phase census of the 110 bp twist-corrected row — how many distinct column phases it has, how many carry eight columns, and which are centro-symmetric — or the statement that a non-uniform pitch has no phase variable | A8.2 | **DONE** (iteration 32) — claim [`C-0136`](gpd/claims/C-0136-mixed-domain-phase-and-honeycomb-twist.md), task [`T-216`](gpd/tasks/T-216-mixed-domain-phase-lattice.md), result `gpd/results/T-216-mixed-domain-phase-lattice.json`, model `src/main/kotlin/structure/LatticePhaseCensus.kt`, study `structure.MixedDomainPhaseLatticeStudyKt`. **THE SECOND BRANCH OF THE ACCEPTANCE, AND IT HOLDS AT 112 bp TOO: a SEAMLESS row has no translational phase variable at all.** Its two ends *are* the tile edges and both carry a scaffold crossover, so both end columns are pinned and the admissible rigid translation group is **`{0}`** — enumerated over every base-pair translation, 219 at 110 bp and 223 at 112. **So `C-0090`'s *"ten eight-column phases collapse to two"* is a collapse from a TRANSLATION to a PARITY**: at 38.08 nm phases 8 and 24 give **identical column positions** to `1e−12` nm with **inverted parities** at all eight columns, and `C-0015`'s 32-phase sweep is a freedom of a tile whose row ends are *not* crossovers (10 of 32 carry eight columns at the nominal 40.00 nm, reproduced). The binary exchanges every duplex's `EAST`/`WEST` azimuth — **which face the arms point out of** — 52 stations against 53, and `C-0090` measures it without naming it (0.0621469105 against 0.070693794). **What the mixed row GAINS is an arrangement axis: 21 members against the uniform row's one**, every one carrying **eight columns as an identity** (`domains + 1`) where the uniform lattice's count is a function of the phase; 3 with centro-symmetric columns, 12 up to reflection, **42 column lattices** against the 112 bp row's two. **And a centro-symmetric COLUMN set is not a centro-symmetric STATION lattice** — `C-0063`'s family needs the second: **6 arrangements** have one under some offset convention, **7** enumerable `(arrangement, convention)` lattices where selecting on column symmetry finds three, and **3 need no mirroring**, so they carry **no 30° station**. **Sixteen exhaustive enumerations**: `C-0133`'s recommended lattice is confirmed flattest under the graded field (**0.0580196384**, reproduced at `8.5e−10`) and **displaced at zero prestrain** (0.0552788 on another arrangement); the best 30°-free lattice dishes **0.0629599**, **1.085×**, inside `T-5b`; the parity binary is worth **1.295×** under the field against `C-0090`'s **1.13752709** on the uniform row; and the two arrangements admitting a **24-rise** arm are exactly the two whose family collapses `163 296 → 11 664` and which fail `T-5b`. Raises [`CH-0164`](gpd/challenges/CH-0164-a-seamless-row-has-a-parity-not-a-phase.md). Spawns `T-219`. |
| T-217 | **Can the four-layer HONEYCOMB tile be twist-corrected?** | The same integer arithmetic on the honeycomb's own ladder — 7 bp between adjacent azimuths, **21 bp** per azimuth (`C-0119`) against 10.5 bp/turn — giving the admissible row lengths, the residual invariant and whether it is the same quarter base pair | A8.2 | **DONE** (iteration 32) — claim [`C-0136`](gpd/claims/C-0136-mixed-domain-phase-and-honeycomb-twist.md), task [`T-217`](gpd/tasks/T-217-honeycomb-twist-correction.md), result `gpd/results/T-217-honeycomb-twist-correction.json`, study `structure.HoneycombTwistCorrectionStudyKt`. **THE CHEAP BOUND CLOSED IT IN ONE DIVISION AND THE ANSWER IS FAVOURABLE.** caDNAno lays the honeycomb's 21 bp azimuth period out as **two turns**, so `ω_d = 720/21 = 34.2857 °/bp` — which **IS** B-DNA's `360/10.5`, the same number. `Δω` is **exactly zero**: the honeycomb has nothing to correct, and `C-0104`'s 15.4497275° threshold, `C-0107`'s 17.15–24.98° and every register number in `C-0133` are **square-lattice** numbers with driver zero there. **One fact unifies both tasks**: `10.5 = 21/2`, so `h` half turns is an integer number of base pairs **iff `h ≡ 0 (mod 4)`** and the distance is exactly **0.25** for odd `h` — `C-0133`'s theorem is the odd case, the honeycomb's period is **four**. **The connectivity half does not transfer and was DERIVED**: scaffold crossovers sit at `7j ± 5 (mod 21)`, so `N ≡ 7Δ + {0,10,11} (mod 21)` with `Δ = (b−a) mod 3 ≠ 0` — and the *same* construction on the square sheet's own azimuths returns `N ≡ 16 (mod 32)`, i.e. **16, 48, 80, 112, 144, 176**, `C-0086`'s list exactly. The honeycomb's width list is **4.571× denser** (one every **7.00 bp** against 32) and reaches §3's nominal far closer: **119 bp = 40.46 nm, +1.15 %** against the square sheet's 38.08 nm at **−4.80 %** and `C-0133`'s 37.40 nm at **−6.50 %**. **The quarter base pair relocates and stops accumulating** — caDNAno's 5 bp scaffold offset against the exact 5.25 is **0.25 bp = 8.5714°**, `C-0133`'s invariant, but **local** where the square sheet's is global. **And the whole reading rests on ONE constant**: at 10.44 bp/turn the honeycomb accumulates **22.07°** over 112 bp, **36.8 %** of the square sheet's 60.0°, and at 10.34 it equals it. **No solve was justified** — this repository's lattice machinery is single-layer square-lattice throughout. Raises [`CH-0165`](gpd/challenges/CH-0165-an-integral-scaffold-lattice-is-necessary-not-sufficient.md). Spawns `T-218`, `T-219`. |
| T-218 | **Which turn sense `Δ` does a caDNAno `15 × 4` honeycomb x-raster carry?** | The neighbour-class sequence of the scaffold path through the honeycomb cross-section — i.e. `Δ = (b − a) mod 3` at each helix — or the statement that it alternates | A8.2 | **DONE** (iteration 33) — claim [`C-0140`](gpd/claims/C-0140-honeycomb-raster-turn-sense.md), task [`T-218`](gpd/tasks/T-218-honeycomb-raster-turn-sense.md), result `gpd/results/T-218-honeycomb-raster-turn-sense.json`, study `structure.HoneycombRasterTurnSenseStudyKt`. **IT ALTERNATES, AND THE CHEAP BOUND IS A THEOREM.** A honeycomb path can never continue in the same direction, so an x-raster row is **corrugated** — which is what the caDNAno paper says in one sentence (*"they stagger up and down and encompass helices that are actually at two different y-positions"*) — consecutive helices are on **opposite sublattices**, and `Δ_geom` alternates. The scaffold runs the full length of every helix so its **axial direction** alternates too, and `Δ_eff = (s·Δ_geom) mod 3`. **The two alternations cancel WITHIN a row and the `m − 1` row turns break them**: every row interior carries one sense, consecutive rows carry opposite senses, and design (i) ends at **sense 2 on 30 helices and sense 1 on 28** of the 58 with a defined sense. **So neither 112 bp nor 119 bp is a uniform width** — `C-0119`'s 112 bp is admissible on 28 of 58 and `C-0136`'s 40.46 nm on the other 30, and 0 of 2 100 widths serve both. **The remedy costs 3 bp = 1.02 nm**: at **112 / 108 bp** the tile's axial extent is **116 bp = 39.44 nm, `−1.40 %`** of §3's nominal, on 6 596 nt inside M13, faces ragged by 4 and 8 bp — **better than the square lattice's `−4.80 %`** and `C-0133`'s `−6.50 %`. **The only constant-sense raster has ONE row**, which is two `y` positions and not four layers. **The square lattice's unconditionality is an accident of `4 = 2 × 2`** — its in-plane pair is two azimuth classes and `2` is its own negative mod 4 — reproduced here on the same code as `C-0086`'s `N ≡ 16 (mod 32)`. **And the built blocks need none of it: their raster turns are 28 unpaired nucleotides**, `126 = 98 + 28` per helix, `60 × 126 = 7 560` exactly. **PASS on all six predicates; none of the nine falsifiers fired, including `F3`, declared open.** Raises [`CH-0172`](gpd/challenges/CH-0172-a-honeycomb-x-raster-carries-both-turn-senses.md) and [`CH-0173`](gpd/challenges/CH-0173-the-built-block-turns-on-loops-not-crossovers.md); spawns `T-230`, `T-231` (numbered past the next free slot so a concurrent sibling cannot collide). |
| T-219 | **The honeycomb's own station lattice, plan ceiling and placement family** | An out-of-plane station census, an arm/plan ceiling and a centro-symmetric placement family on the honeycomb lattice, or the statement of what a single-layer square-lattice model cannot supply | A8.2 | **DONE** (iteration 33) — claim [`C-0141`](gpd/claims/C-0141-honeycomb-station-lattice-and-placement.md), task [`T-219`](gpd/tasks/T-219-honeycomb-station-lattice-and-placement.md), result `gpd/results/T-219-honeycomb-station-lattice-and-placement.json`, model `src/main/kotlin/tile/HoneycombFaceLattice.kt`, study `tile.HoneycombPlacementStudyKt`, tests `src/test/kotlin/tile/HoneycombFaceLatticeTest.kt` (23, written first and watched to fail). **ALL THREE DELIVERABLES EXIST — AND THE CHEAP BOUND FOUND THAT THE CROSS-SECTION THEY LIVE ON IS NOT A HONEYCOMB.** One multiplication: a honeycomb spends `3√3/4 · d²` = **8.35449857 nm²** of plan per helix and the assumed cross-section spends `d²` = 6.431296, **1.29903811×**, because the in-plane row pitch is `3d/2` and the layer pitch `d√3/2` and only their **product** is the cell — so `CLAUDE.md`'s standing *"stack rows at `d√3/2`"* fixes one factor and makes the density error **worse** (1.50× against 1.30×). Every four-layer `edgeY` is exactly **1.5×** too small, and because `10 × 1.5 = 15` the corrected `10 × 6` carries **38.04 nm**, the number the corpus gave `15 × 4`: **the footprint ordering REVERSES** — `15 × 4` is 56.524 nm across, **1.40084263** of §3's 40.35, and `10 × 6` is 37.504 nm, **0.929467162** ([`CH-0174`](gpd/challenges/CH-0174-the-four-layer-cross-section-is-not-a-honeycomb.md)). **CENSUS**: `C-0122`'s **90** and **60** reproduce at departure `0.0` and every reason given for them is withdrawn — the face gives every one of its `m` helices **exactly one** rooting azimuth at **30°**, sign alternating, and **no perpendicular root exists anywhere on the face**; `CH-0151`'s 132/90 belongs to a half-row termination none of the seven published designs has, and `C-0128`'s rigid-body oblique cost falls **6.017× → 2.67233333×** ([`CH-0175`](gpd/challenges/CH-0175-the-face-azimuth-is-thirty-degrees-and-there-is-one-of-it.md)). **PLAN CEILING**: the exact bisection at ten counts — 38.08 / 16.66 / 9.52 / 5.872 / 4.604 / **2.38** nm as the count saturates — reproducing `C-0072`'s **9.535 nm** on the square phase-24 lattice at departure `0.0` through the same function. `C-0069`'s 8.16439083 nm element is afforded to **45 paths on `15 × 4`** and **30 on `10 × 6`**. **PLACEMENT FAMILY**: the 7 bp inter-row stagger is **FORCED** (two bond classes, residues 7 or 14 mod 21, never 0), so the **rooting-helix parity** decides everything — `15 × 4` admits **no** centro-symmetric station lattice at any of 21 phases, either offset, either row length; `10 × 6` admits one **at the full 60 stations**, with >20 000 members. **WHAT THE SQUARE LATTICE CANNOT SUPPLY** is named function by function — and the useful half is that `maximumPlanCeilingForCount`, `rootedLengthCeiling`, `rowRootOptions`, `armDirections` and `centroSymmetricPlacementsOn` all take an **explicit** lattice and took the honeycomb one **unmodified**; what is square-lattice-specific is the site **generator**, `centroSymmetricUpwardPhases`, `CrossoverLayout` and the grillage's single pitch. **AND THE GEOMETRY MOVES A VERDICT**: re-solved with only `edgeY` and the two pitches changed (`C-0120`'s 0.0577199433 and 0.00874363524 reproduce at `0.0`), `15 × 4` dishes **0.0978155002** with `C-0116`'s threshold at **0.276970522** — **inside** the measured 0.26–0.33 band, so at `f = 0.26` it dishes **0.101759944** and **fails `T-5b`** — while `10 × 6` dishes 0.0240648102, threshold 0.012737738, flat across the whole band. **PASS on all four predicates; `F1`, `F2`, `F4`, `F5` did not fire and `F3` FIRED favourably** (the inboard bound binds only at saturation; a denser ladder is a larger choice set). Raises `CH-0174`, `CH-0175`; spawns `T-232`, `T-233`. |

The one-line generalisation: **a percentage is an error bar on the thing its DENOMINATOR is, and a quantity is exposed to it only if it is homogeneous of degree one in that thing** — a margin is not (pinned), a ratio is not (cancels), a force is. ~~TODO — **MEDIUM, and it is a scope repair rather than a number repair.** Raised by [`CH-0167`](gpd/challenges/CH-0167-the-123-214-per-cent-is-a-level-and-it-is-quoted-as-an-error-bar-on-a-stiffness.md). The census is **66 occurrences in 31 claims**, plus 8 in `ANSWERS.md`, 10 in `TASKS.md` and 6 in `DECISIONS-FOR-NDI.md`. `C-0137` shows the ratio is an error bar on a **level** and the quantities it is attached to are **stiffnesses read at a force-pinned point**, where the level is absorbed into the bias. **The partition matters and is the whole of the work**: a banner attached to a *free-tile* quantity (a blocking force, `C-0021`'s well depth) is quoting the **right** error bar and must be left alone; the challenge is against **held**, load-line quantities. | T-221 | **Which wall is a planar coupling criterion owed at — the bare duplex or the charge-saturated gap face?** | A stated rule, with its ground, for whether `Ξ` and `μ_GC` in a planar wall-wall criterion are read from the duplex cylinder (`Ξ = 24.0`) or from the smeared saturated gap-facing wall (`Ξ = 1.46`) — or the statement that both must be carried | A7.4 | **DONE** (iteration 34) — claim [`C-0143`](gpd/claims/C-0143-planar-coupling-wall.md), raising [`CH-0178`](gpd/challenges/CH-0178-a-criterion-with-no-root-returned-its-own-bracket-floor.md) and [`CH-0179`](gpd/challenges/CH-0179-exponentially-large-is-not-a-property-of-the-branch.md). **The rule: BARE, never renormalised — and within the bare family the geometry is a `3.58x` BRACKET the verdict is invariant across.** The cheap bound ran first and reframed the question: `Xi` and `D̃` are BOTH linear in `sigma`, so `Xi/D̃ = q²l_B/D` carries no wall convention and Eq. (64) is exactly `ln(D/mu) < D/(q²l_B)` — the disputed `16.495x` enters once, as `ln = 2.8031`. Swept as a `2 x 2`, **the verdict is constant down the CHARGE column and not across the GEOMETRY row**: at 5, 7 and 10 nm all four bare readings fail (`Xi = 24.00 / 57.99 / 85.85 / 171.70`) and both renormalised ones pass (`2.8564 / 1.4548`), because the criterion at 7 nm admits `Xi < 4.7317`, i.e. `sigma < 0.18459 e/nm²`. **The `16.5x` is 100 % the renormalisation and 0 % the cylinder/plane axis it was framed as.** Ground: Kanduc's Eq. (14) fixes the counterion count by ELECTRONEUTRALITY against `sigma_1 + sigma_2`, so a renormalised `sigma` deletes exactly the population `Xi` counts; `mu` is a Gauss's-law distance; the contact-value theorem is exact beyond mean field on the bare `sigma`; and a saturated `sigma_eff` is an OUTPUT of the theory the criterion tests. **The corpus had already chosen**: Kanduc Eq. (64) IS Naji Eq. (20), `T-6` has emitted it at the bare duplex since iteration 3 (`13.5177 nm`, reproduced to `0.0`), and adopting the saturated reading would require retracting `C-0005`'s `123–214 %`. **But Eq. (64) is the REPULSIVE branch and this gap attracts**, so Eq. (65) is owed; derived here, attraction needs `D̃ > (1+zeta)/\|zeta\|`, the bound's infimum sits AT that boundary at `0.94–1.15x` Eq. (64)'s, and the excluded sliver is **0.23–1.10 %** of an asymmetry range — `CH-0179`. **No verdict moves**: `C-0137` measured the same `16x` as `1.44 %` of `C-0017`'s margin. All six declared falsifiers checked, **none fired**. ~~TODO — **MEDIUM, and it is the one thing `C-0137` explicitly does not settle.**~~ `CLAUDE.md` says *"read `Ξ` from the duplex cylinder charge density, not the projected one"* — and that is written for the **local** coupling, where the projected density gives a PB contact density 89× past close packing. Kanduč et al.'s Eq. (64) is a criterion for a **planar wall bounding a slit**, and this device's gap-facing wall is charge-saturated (`C-0008`: three charge readings spanning 3× give `σ_eff` within 7 %). Evaluated at 7 nm the criterion gives a bound of **14.43** against `Ξ = 24.00` (**FAILS**) and **2.80** against `Ξ = 1.455` (**PASSES**) — 16.5× apart, opposite sides of one inequality, and the answer decides whether the actuated gap is inside the loop expansion's validity range at all. Every placement, phase, plan-ceiling and flatness number in this corpus is a property of a set of Kotlin constants; when the cross-section turned out not to be a honeycomb, that made the results **invalid rather than re-runnable**. Its scaffold occupies bases **14 → 140** (126, the allotment) and its staples **28 → 126** (98, the paired count), so the scaffold **turns at the rim with no topological loopout** and the two **DUPLEX** ends it joins are **28 nt apart, 14 on each side**: the covalent link sits **`14 bp = 4.76 nm` OUTBOARD** of the duplex end, and what stands between the two rim nodes is **ssDNA**. **The loops' stated purpose is aggregation, not closure** — *"Unpaired scaffold bases **often** are introduced at the ends of helices **to minimize undesired multimerization**"*, in the honeycomb paper and again in its square-lattice sibling, which adds the seam alternative and a **90–97 %** paired range against the honeycomb blocks' **77.8 %** — and the field's 2024 tutorial calls a `4+` base poly-T loop **on the staples** *"tried-and-tested"*, at **zero scaffold**. **What the 28 nt BUYS is freedom from caDNAno's `±5 bp` residue condition, because an unpaired base has no azimuth** — which is how a lattice carrying both turn senses folds at ONE uniform length, and exactly why `C-0151`'s zero-loop raster must be two-length. **The two designs are separated by the SCAFFOLD**: on the drawable `102 / 109` raster the built allowance needs **8 010 nt**, so M13mp18 is short by **761**, p7560 by **450** and p8064 has **54** spare; inverted, M13 affords **15 nt** per turn, p7560 **20**, p8064 **exactly 28**. **And a tether is not free**: at 15 nt the turn carries `2.03800431–3.03288672 pN` and stores `1.00857129–1.48373364 k_BT`, so 59 of them store `59.5057061–87.5402845 k_BT` — **`7.15756436–10.5296662×`** `C-0190`'s whole route-A ceiling and `7.43849502–10.9429501` crossover columns of the host sheet. **The existence result is NEGATIVE and recorded so it can be refuted**: 30 queries in 9 named families, 348 records, **186 unique**, **7** naming the honeycomb lattice, **0** reporting the motif. **So `C-0175` §9, `C-0180` §4 and `C-0190` are correct about a DRAWABLE design nobody has folded**, and the conditionality is quantified: the free tile is `0.0501417316` untied against `0.0446459684` tied (**both inside `T-5b`**, so `F7` did not fire at the free-tile level), `C-0180`'s **2 of 64** becomes `C-0167`'s **0 of 64**, `CH-0228`'s `0.0764244991` ceiling and `C-0190`'s `0.296735462`, `8.31368089 k_BT` and `17.1428571°` all cease to exist, and `C-0151`'s raster stops being a **selection**. Twelve reproductions, worst departure **`2.5e−9`** (the reproduced file's own emission precision); the FJC mirror asserted against `T-230`'s committed records at that precision — `round9(mine)` **is** the committed literal at **252 of 252** fields; 24 named assertions, 0 failures; result file **byte-identical across two independent runs**. **No Kotlin main source was touched**: the emitter is Python, in `tools/`. ~~**TODO — HIGH**, raised by [`C-0190`](gpd/claims/C-0190-the-departure-is-common-mode-and-what-replaces-it.md) (`T-291`) §10. Selecting the row-faithful count moves `C-0146`'s verdict to its **116 bp / 11-column** column — **3 flat cells of 8**, with `C-0142`'s tightest cell (3 columns rim-graded, **0.100357905**) lost across `T-5b`'s 0.10 and the **recommended cell (1 column, 10 paths, equal springs) flat at every reading**. Five uncoupled dishing states **re-solved** and reproducing `C-0146` at departures **`1.9e−10` to `2.0e−9`**. `F2`, `F3`, `F4` did not fire; **`F1` FIRED**, at 2 of 12 row-derived readings — both of them the strictest reading at 102 / 109, whose 32.30 nm window clears nine pitches by the same **0.07 nm**, a second instance rather than a counter-example — and **0 of 6 at 112 / 108**, the pair every graded cell is read at. **`EDGE_MARGIN`'s KDoc is REPLACED** by the condition `(lengthX − 2·margin) mod columnSpacing`, with both failures named and pointers to `columnSlack`, `guardIsInert` and `crossoverColumnsIn`. **`CH-0185` is ANSWERED.** |
| T-244 | **Read caDNAno's face-sublattice crossover bond-class residues, which now fix a station COUNT** | Which of the 7 or 14 bp inter-row ladder offsets a honeycomb face carries, from the primary rules rather than swept | A8.2 | **DONE** (iteration 36) — claim [`C-0148`](gpd/claims/C-0148-face-bond-class-residues-and-row-span-columns.md), task [`T-244`](gpd/tasks/T-244-face-bond-class-residues.md), result `gpd/results/T-244-face-bond-class-residues.json`, model `src/main/kotlin/tile/HoneycombBondClassResidues.kt`, study `tile.HoneycombBondClassStudyKt`. **THE OFFSET IS 14, AND THE PHASE IS NOT FREE EITHER.** Three numbers out of one paragraph of the caDNAno paper (`PMC2731887`, already local, **read directly**) settle it with no solve: 21 bp per pair, **7 bp per class step**, and — never used in this repository before — **±5 bp** for a scaffold crossover. A face helix's free azimuth is bond class **0** on sublattice `A` (330°) and class **1** on `B` (30°), and the raster's row parity puts `B` on even rows, so the offset is `−7 ≡ **14**` in `TwoLengthRaster.stationLattice`'s own parameterisation — at **32 of 32** proper readings (both cross-sections, both axial signs, both faces, both length pairs, and the proper rotation about `y`). The one reading that returns 7 is a cross-section mirror **without** the axial reversal, i.e. an improper transformation of a chiral object. The map also re-derives `C-0136`'s `{0, 10, 11}` length rule from an independent construction. **The `±5` rule then OVER-determines the phase**: one lattice constant `b₀` must serve every raster crossover, and at `C-0140`'s recommended **112 / 108** none does — **10 of 59** raster crossovers would have to be *forced*. Exactly **one** of `C-0140`'s five candidate pairs closes, **102 / 109**, where `b₀ = 5`, the phase is **16**, the offset is 14 and the face carries **55 of 60** stations (82 of 90 on `15 × 4`) — which is **also the sweep's own maximum**. **`CH-0184`'s saturating pair (phase 11 / 14 bp, 60 of 60) is reproduced at departure `0.0` and WITHDRAWN as unbuildable**, so a **six-column placement does not stand at any pair examined**. **None of `F1`–`F5` fired** — `F5` is written the favourable way round (*"the 112 / 108 raster satisfies the scaffold rule at every crossover"*), so its **not** firing is the finding. **`CH-0184` is ANSWERED.** Raises [`CH-0188`](gpd/challenges/CH-0188-the-recommended-raster-does-not-close.md) against `C-0140`'s 112 / 108 recommendation and [`CH-0189`](gpd/challenges/CH-0189-the-ladder-phase-is-not-a-sweep.md) against the 21-phase ladder sweep. |
| T-245 | **Re-select the honeycomb two-length row pair on SCAFFOLD CLOSURE, and re-grade the coupled cells there** | A length pair that closes on caDNAno's `±5 bp` scaffold-crossover rule, chosen among the closing family rather than among `C-0140`'s five, with `C-0146`'s eight `10 × 6` cells re-graded at its own row-derived crossover-column count and its own determined station lattice | A8.2 | **DONE** — [`C-0151`](gpd/claims/C-0151-closing-raster-selection.md), result [`gpd/results/T-245-closing-raster-selection.json`](gpd/results/T-245-closing-raster-selection.json). **The cheap bound settled the family with no solve**: closure depends on the two lengths only through their residues modulo 21, three pairs close — `(7, 14)`, `(17, 3)`, `(18, 4)`, identical at both cross-sections — and **all three have `L₁ − L₂ ≡ 14 (mod 21)`**, so the least stagger a **drawable** raster can carry is **7 bp**. `C-0140`'s *"at most 4 bp"* filter is therefore **exactly disjoint** from closure ([`CH-0194`](gpd/challenges/CH-0194-the-filter-and-closure-are-disjoint.md)). Selected inside the closing family, **`102 / 109 bp`** is jointly best on extent (116 bp = 39.44 nm, `−1.40 %` — the *same* extent as the pair it replaces) and strictly best among the three that tie there (10 columns against 9 and 6, 55 stations against 50 and 40). The two pairs give the **same tile**, so the whole cost of closure on the flatness axis is the interface window and **one crossover column**: **2 flat cells of 8** against 3, and the recommended cell survives at both ends of the measured band (`0.0773373597` at `f = 0.30`, `0.0821458169` at `f = 0.26`). Ladder phase **determined at 16**; closure buys 270 nt of scaffold and takes the blunt-end stacking clearance from 0.18 rises to 3.18. **`CH-0187`, `CH-0188` and `CH-0189` are ANSWERED**; raises [`CH-0195`](gpd/challenges/CH-0195-both-graded-column-counts-belong-to-an-undrawable-raster.md) — both column counts this corpus has graded belong to a raster that cannot be drawn |
| T-246 | **Price a FORCED scaffold crossover, or record that this repository cannot** | Either a published yield or stability cost for a caDNAno forced scaffold crossover — the departure a 112 / 108 raster needs at 10 of 59 of its crossovers — or a recorded negative existence result with its query strings, so that `CH-0188`'s verdict can be read as a cost rather than as a binary | A8.2 | **DONE** (iteration 37) — claim [`C-0152`](gpd/claims/C-0152-forced-scaffold-crossover-price.md), task [`T-246`](gpd/tasks/T-246-forced-scaffold-crossover-price.md), result `gpd/results/T-246-forced-scaffold-crossover-price.json`, model `src/main/kotlin/tile/ForcedCrossoverPrice.kt`, study `tile.ForcedCrossoverPriceStudyKt`, tests `src/test/kotlin/tile/ForcedCrossoverPriceTest.kt` (25, written first and watched fail); literature corpus and every raw response in `gpd/data/T-246-sources/`. **`CH-0188`'S SEVERITY IS NOW STATED: THE CEILING IS `0.350894669 k_BT` PER FORCED CROSSOVER — SUB-THERMAL — AND `0.438634952` OF ONE HOST-SHEET CROSSOVER COLUMN FOR ALL TEN.** The cheap bound decided it and it turned on an arithmetic nobody had run: the departure is an **AZIMUTH**, and because one turn is **10.5** bp the smallest one the 21-residue lattice offers is **half** a base-pair step — **17.1428571°**, at a displacement of **ten or eleven** base pairs, not one — this raster's stray residue being **10 bp** from an allowed one and 1 bp from the other, and the azimuth taking the ten — so *the cheapest forcing is the one displaced furthest in base pairs*. That span is **0.962320075 nm**, **`+8.2360318 σ`** of `T-71`'s measured phosphodiester step and **1.27165741×** its 99th percentile, so a forced crossover does **not** close as a bond at rigid geometry; nor can the axes pay alone at 1 bp, where the smallest span reachable at **any** separation is 1.02370786 nm. The price is therefore elastic, and since the structure minimises over channels **any** admissible channel is a ceiling: the rigid-duplex limit of `C-0104`'s own roll mapping is **one multiplication**. **No published price exists** — 68 queries in 7 families, 14 papers examined, 13 **read directly**, every raw response retained — and it is an **explicit exclusion** rather than a null search: caDNAno's own Discussion says *"additional software development will be required … for caDNAno to **predict the structural consequences of these changes**"* and *"more work is also needed to see what design rules lead to stable structures"*, unanswered for seventeen years, and the gallery it points to for yields carries none, live or at its 2012 capture. So the answer is `P-6`'s **ceiling and threshold**, and **the lattice quantises the threshold**: the next rung up (1 bp, 34.2857143°) costs **10.5268401 `k_BT`** for the block, **1.31590485×** the calibration, so the departure this raster needs sits a **factor of two below the first rung that would exceed the fold's own currency**. The one published sweep on the same axis (Ke et al., *Chem. Sci.* 2012, **read directly**) converts to **34.29° / 68.57° / 102.86°** of register per interface: its bottom rung is **twice** this departure and it folds — **ambiguously**, improving a 60hb while degrading a 24hb — and 102.86° *"abolished productive folding completely"*. **And the lattice supplies its own calibration for free**: at exact 10.5 bp/turn an **ALLOWED** scaffold crossover already carries **8.57142857°** (`+3.39051453 σ`, *outside* P99), which every honeycomb origami ever folded absorbs — a forced one adds exactly **twice** it. **None of `F1`–`F5` fired**; `F3` and `F5` are written the favourable way round, so their **not** firing is the finding in both cases. Two runs **byte-identical**; `CH-0188`'s 10-of-59, 8-of-59 and 0 reproduced at departure `0.0`. **What is still not priced is named rather than assumed**: an elastic energy is not a folding yield, and the **flatness** channel cannot be run here at all — the departure is `1.10958961×` `C-0104`'s 15.4497275° threshold, but that threshold is a **uniform** prestrain on a **single-layer square-lattice** tile and `OrigamiGrillage` never reads `layers`. So scaffold closure is a **reason to prefer 102 / 109**, not a proof that 112 / 108 is unbuildable, and `C-0148`'s *"buildable and off-rule"* is upheld as a measurement. Raises [`CH-0196`](gpd/challenges/CH-0196-the-eight-base-pair-yield-cost-is-an-elided-attribution.md) — **Ke et al.'s 8 bp yield cost is an elided attribution**, the paper declining it in the very next sentence (*"irrespective of the position of the breaks"*) and deferring to *"future systematic studies"*, so the nearest priced analogue in this corpus is **not priced** — and [`CH-0197`](gpd/challenges/CH-0197-an-allowed-scaffold-crossover-is-not-aligned.md) against `C-0147`'s aligned `n = 0` reading. |
